```
    22→// AgentRunner abstracts invoking a coding agent for e2e tests.
    23→// This follows the multi-agent pattern from cmd/entire/cli/agent/agent.go.
    24→type AgentRunner interface {
    25→	// Name returns the agent name (e.g., "claude-code", "gemini-cli")
    26→	Name() string
    27→
    28→	// IsAvailable checks if the agent CLI is installed and authenticated
    29→	IsAvailable() (bool, error)
    30→
    31→	// RunPrompt executes a prompt and returns the result
    32→	RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error)
    33→
    34→	// RunPromptWithTools executes with specific allowed tools
    35→	RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error)
    36→}

    38→// AgentResult holds the result of an agent invocation.
    39→type AgentResult struct {
    40→	Stdout   string
    41→	Stderr   string
    42→	ExitCode int
    43→	Duration time.Duration
    44→}
    45→
    46→// AgentRunnerConfig holds configuration for agent runners.

    52→// NewAgentRunner creates an agent runner based on the agent name.
    53→//

    55→func NewAgentRunner(name string, config AgentRunnerConfig) AgentRunner {

    59→	case AgentNameGemini:
    60→		return NewGeminiCLIRunner(config)
    61→	default:

    63→		return &unavailableRunner{name: name}

    92→
    93→// NewClaudeCodeRunner creates a new Claude Code runner with the given config.
    94→func NewClaudeCodeRunner(config AgentRunnerConfig) *ClaudeCodeRunner {
    95→	model := config.Model
    96→	if model == "" {
    97→		model = os.Getenv("E2E_CLAUDE_MODEL")
    98→		if model == "" {
    99→			model = "haiku"
   100→		}
   101→	}
   102→
   103→	timeout := config.Timeout
   104→	if timeout == 0 {
   105→		if envTimeout := os.Getenv("E2E_TIMEOUT"); envTimeout != "" {
   106→			if parsed, err := time.ParseDuration(envTimeout); err == nil {
   107→				timeout = parsed
   108→			}
   109→		}
   110→		if timeout == 0 {
   111→			timeout = 2 * time.Minute
   112→		}
   113→	}
   114→
   115→	return &ClaudeCodeRunner{
   116→		Model:        model,
   117→		Timeout:      timeout,
   118→		AllowedTools: []string{"Edit", "Read", "Write", "Bash", "Glob", "Grep"},
   119→	}
   120→}
   121→
   122→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) Name() string {
   123→	return AgentNameClaudeCode
   124→}
   125→
   126→// IsAvailable checks if Claude CLI is installed and responds to --version.
   127→// Note: This does NOT verify authentication status. Claude Code uses OAuth
   128→// authentication (via `claude login`), not ANTHROPIC_API_KEY. If the CLI is
   129→// installed but not logged in, tests will fail at RunPrompt time.
   130→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) IsAvailable() (bool, error) {
   131→	// Check if claude CLI is in PATH
   132→	if _, err := exec.LookPath("claude"); err != nil {
   133→		return false, fmt.Errorf("claude CLI not found in PATH: %w", err)
   134→	}
   135→
   136→	// Check if claude is working (--version doesn't require auth)
   137→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
   138→	defer cancel()
   139→
   140→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "claude", "--version")
   141→	if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
   142→		return false, fmt.Errorf("claude CLI not working: %w", err)
   143→	}
   144→
   145→	return true, nil
   146→}
   147→
   148→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   149→	return r.RunPromptWithTools(ctx, workDir, prompt, r.AllowedTools)
   150→}
   151→
   152→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   153→	// Build command: claude --model <model> -p "<prompt>" --allowedTools <tools>
   154→	args := []string{
   155→		"--model", r.Model,
   156→		"-p", prompt,
   157→	}
   158→
   159→	if len(tools) > 0 {
   160→		// Claude CLI expects each tool as a separate argument after --allowedTools
   161→		// e.g., --allowedTools Edit Read Bash (not --allowedTools "Edit,Read,Bash")
   162→		args = append(args, "--allowedTools")
   163→		args = append(args, tools...)
   164→	}
   165→
   166→	// Create context with timeout
   167→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, r.Timeout)
   168→	defer cancel()
   169→
   170→	//nolint:gosec // args are constructed from trusted config, not user input
   171→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "claude", args...)
   172→	cmd.Dir = workDir
   173→
   174→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   175→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   176→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   177→
   178→	start := time.Now()
   179→	err := cmd.Run()
   180→	duration := time.Since(start)
   181→
   182→	result := &AgentResult{
   183→		Stdout:   stdout.String(),
   184→		Stderr:   stderr.String(),
   185→		Duration: duration,
   186→	}
   187→
   188→	if err != nil {
   189→		exitErr := &exec.ExitError{}
   190→		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
   191→			result.ExitCode = exitErr.ExitCode()
   192→		} else {
   193→			result.ExitCode = -1
   194→		}
   195→		//nolint:wrapcheck // error is from exec.Run, caller can check ExitCode in result
   196→		return result, err
   197→	}
   198→
   199→	result.ExitCode = 0
   200→	return result, nil
   201→}
   202→
   203→// GeminiCLIRunner implements AgentRunner for Gemini CLI.
   204→// See: https://github.com/google-gemini/gemini-cli
   205→type GeminiCLIRunner struct {
   206→	Model   string
   207→	Timeout time.Duration
   208→}
   209→
   210→// NewGeminiCLIRunner creates a new Gemini CLI runner with the given config.
   211→func NewGeminiCLIRunner(config AgentRunnerConfig) *GeminiCLIRunner {
   212→	model := config.Model
   213→	if model == "" {
   214→		model = os.Getenv("E2E_GEMINI_MODEL")
   215→		// Default to gemini-2.5-flash if not specified
   216→		if model == "" {
   217→			model = "gemini-2.5-flash"
   218→		}
   219→	}
   220→
   221→	timeout := config.Timeout
   222→	if timeout == 0 {
   223→		if envTimeout := os.Getenv("E2E_TIMEOUT"); envTimeout != "" {
   224→			if parsed, err := time.ParseDuration(envTimeout); err == nil {
   225→				timeout = parsed
   226→			}
   227→		}
   228→		if timeout == 0 {
   229→			timeout = 2 * time.Minute
   230→		}
   231→	}
   232→
   233→	return &GeminiCLIRunner{
   234→		Model:   model,
   235→		Timeout: timeout,
   236→	}
   237→}
   238→
   239→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) Name() string {
   240→	return AgentNameGemini
   241→}
   242→
   243→// IsAvailable checks if Gemini CLI is installed and authenticated.
   244→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) IsAvailable() (bool, error) {
   245→	// Check if gemini CLI is in PATH
   246→	if _, err := exec.LookPath("gemini"); err != nil {
   247→		return false, fmt.Errorf("gemini CLI not found in PATH: %w", err)
   248→	}
   249→
   250→	// Check if gemini is working (--version doesn't require auth)
   251→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
   252→	defer cancel()
   253→
   254→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "gemini", "--version")
   255→	if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
   256→		return false, fmt.Errorf("gemini CLI not working: %w", err)
   257→	}
   258→
   259→	return true, nil
   260→}
   261→
   262→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   263→	return r.RunPromptWithTools(ctx, workDir, prompt, nil)
   264→}
   265→
   266→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   267→	// Build command: gemini -m <model> -p "<prompt>" --approval-mode auto_edit --allowed-tools <tools>
   268→	// --approval-mode auto_edit: auto-approves edit tools (write_file, replace) while prompting for others
   269→	// --allowed-tools: comma-separated list of tools that bypass confirmation (e.g., git commands)
   270→	args := []string{
   271→		"-m", r.Model,
   272→		"-p", prompt,
   273→		"--approval-mode", "auto_edit",
   274→	}
   275→
   276→	// Add default git tools that should be allowed without confirmation
   277→	defaultAllowedTools := []string{
   278→		"ShellTool(git status)",
   279→		"ShellTool(git add)",
   280→		"ShellTool(git commit)",
   281→		"ShellTool(git diff)",
   282→		"ShellTool(git log)",
   283→	}
   284→
   285→	// Merge with any additional tools passed in
   286→	allTools := append(defaultAllowedTools, tools...)
   287→	if len(allTools) > 0 {
   288→		args = append(args, "--allowed-tools", strings.Join(allTools, ","))
   289→	}
   290→
   291→	// Create context with timeout
   292→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, r.Timeout)
   293→	defer cancel()
   294→
   295→	//nolint:gosec // args are constructed from trusted config, not user input
   296→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "gemini", args...)
   297→	cmd.Dir = workDir
   298→
   299→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   300→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   301→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   302→
   303→	start := time.Now()
   304→	err := cmd.Run()
   305→	duration := time.Since(start)
   306→
   307→	result := &AgentResult{
   308→		Stdout:   stdout.String(),
   309→		Stderr:   stderr.String(),
   310→		Duration: duration,
   311→	}
   312→
   313→	if err != nil {
   314→		exitErr := &exec.ExitError{}
   315→		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
   316→			result.ExitCode = exitErr.ExitCode()
   317→		} else {
   318→			result.ExitCode = -1
   319→		}
   320→		//nolint:wrapcheck // error is from exec.Run, caller can check ExitCode in result
   321→		return result, err
   322→	}
   323→
   324→	result.ExitCode = 0
   325→	return result, nil
   326→}
```

```
     1→// Package geminicli implements the Agent interface for Gemini CLI.
     2→package geminicli
     3→
     4→import (
     5→	"context"
     6→	"encoding/json"
     7→	"errors"
     8→	"fmt"
     9→	"io"
    10→	"log/slog"
    11→	"os"
    12→	"path/filepath"
    13→	"regexp"
    14→	"time"
    15→
    16→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
    17→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/logging"
    18→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths"
    19→)
    20→
    21→//nolint:gochecknoinits // Agent self-registration is the intended pattern
    22→func init() {
    23→	agent.Register(agent.AgentNameGemini, NewGeminiCLIAgent)
    24→}
    25→
    26→// GeminiCLIAgent implements the Agent interface for Gemini CLI.
    27→//
    28→//nolint:revive // GeminiCLIAgent is clearer than Agent in this context
    29→type GeminiCLIAgent struct{}
    30→
    31→func NewGeminiCLIAgent() agent.Agent {
    32→	return &GeminiCLIAgent{}
    33→}
    34→
    35→// Name returns the agent registry key.
    36→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) Name() agent.AgentName {
    37→	return agent.AgentNameGemini
    38→}
    39→
    40→// Type returns the agent type identifier.
    41→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) Type() agent.AgentType {
    42→	return agent.AgentTypeGemini
    43→}
    44→
    45→// Description returns a human-readable description.
    46→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) Description() string {
    47→	return "Gemini CLI - Google's AI coding assistant"
    48→}
    49→
    50→// DetectPresence checks if Gemini CLI is configured in the repository.
    51→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) DetectPresence() (bool, error) {
    52→	// Get repo root to check for .gemini directory
    53→	// This is needed because the CLI may be run from a subdirectory
    54→	repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
    55→	if err != nil {
    56→		// Not in a git repo, fall back to CWD-relative check
    57→		repoRoot = "."
    58→	}
    59→
    60→	// Check for .gemini directory
    61→	geminiDir := filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".gemini")
    62→	if _, err := os.Stat(geminiDir); err == nil {
    63→		return true, nil
    64→	}
    65→	// Check for .gemini/settings.json
    66→	settingsFile := filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".gemini", "settings.json")
    67→	if _, err := os.Stat(settingsFile); err == nil {
    68→		return true, nil
    69→	}
    70→	return false, nil
    71→}
    72→
    73→// GetHookConfigPath returns the path to Gemini's hook config file.
    74→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) GetHookConfigPath() string {
    75→	return ".gemini/settings.json"
    76→}
    77→
    78→// SupportsHooks returns true as Gemini CLI supports lifecycle hooks.
    79→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) SupportsHooks() bool {
    80→	return true
    81→}
    82→
    83→// ParseHookInput parses Gemini CLI hook input from stdin.
    84→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) ParseHookInput(hookType agent.HookType, reader io.Reader) (*agent.HookInput, error) {
    85→	data, err := io.ReadAll(reader)
    86→	if err != nil {
    87→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read input: %w", err)
    88→	}
    89→
    90→	if len(data) == 0 {
    91→		return nil, errors.New("empty input")
    92→	}
    93→
    94→	input := &agent.HookInput{
    95→		HookType:  hookType,
    96→		Timestamp: time.Now(),
    97→		RawData:   make(map[string]interface{}),
    98→	}
    99→
   100→	// Parse based on hook type
   101→	switch hookType {
   102→	case agent.HookSessionStart, agent.HookSessionEnd, agent.HookStop:
   103→		var raw sessionInfoRaw
   104→		if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &raw); err != nil {
   105→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse session info: %w", err)
   106→		}
   107→		input.SessionID = raw.SessionID
   108→		input.SessionRef = raw.TranscriptPath
   109→		// Store Gemini-specific fields in RawData
   110→		input.RawData["cwd"] = raw.Cwd
   111→		input.RawData["hook_event_name"] = raw.HookEventName
   112→		if raw.Source != "" {
   113→			input.RawData["source"] = raw.Source
   114→		}
   115→		if raw.Reason != "" {
   116→			input.RawData["reason"] = raw.Reason
   117→		}
   118→
   119→	case agent.HookUserPromptSubmit:
   120→		// BeforeAgent is Gemini's equivalent to Claude's UserPromptSubmit
   121→		// It provides the user's prompt in the "prompt" field
   122→		var raw agentHookInputRaw
   123→		if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &raw); err != nil {
   124→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse agent hook input: %w", err)
   125→		}
   126→		input.SessionID = raw.SessionID
   127→		input.SessionRef = raw.TranscriptPath
   128→		input.RawData["cwd"] = raw.Cwd
   129→		input.RawData["hook_event_name"] = raw.HookEventName
   130→		if raw.Prompt != "" {
   131→			input.UserPrompt = raw.Prompt
   132→			input.RawData["prompt"] = raw.Prompt
   133→		}
   134→
   135→	case agent.HookPreToolUse, agent.HookPostToolUse:
   136→		var raw toolHookInputRaw
   137→		if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &raw); err != nil {
   138→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse tool hook input: %w", err)
   139→		}
   140→		input.SessionID = raw.SessionID
   141→		input.SessionRef = raw.TranscriptPath
   142→		input.ToolName = raw.ToolName
   143→		input.ToolInput = raw.ToolInput
   144→		if hookType == agent.HookPostToolUse {
   145→			input.ToolResponse = raw.ToolResponse
   146→		}
   147→		input.RawData["cwd"] = raw.Cwd
   148→		input.RawData["hook_event_name"] = raw.HookEventName
   149→	}
   150→
   151→	return input, nil
   152→}
   153→
   154→// GetSessionID extracts the session ID from hook input.
   155→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) GetSessionID(input *agent.HookInput) string {
   156→	return input.SessionID
   157→}
   158→
   159→// ProtectedDirs returns directories that Gemini uses for config/state.
   160→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) ProtectedDirs() []string { return []string{".gemini"} }
   161→
   162→// ResolveSessionFile returns the path to a Gemini session file.
   163→// Gemini names files as session-<date>-<shortid>.json where shortid is the first 8 chars
   164→// of the session UUID. This searches for an existing file matching the pattern, falling
   165→// back to <dir>/<id>.json if no match is found.
   166→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) ResolveSessionFile(sessionDir, agentSessionID string) string {
   167→	// Try to find existing file matching Gemini's naming convention:
   168→	// session-*-<first8chars>.json
   169→	shortID := agentSessionID
   170→	if len(shortID) > 8 {
   171→		shortID = shortID[:8]
   172→	}
   173→
   174→	pattern := filepath.Join(sessionDir, "session-*-"+shortID+".json")
   175→	matches, err := filepath.Glob(pattern)
   176→	if err == nil && len(matches) > 0 {
   177→		// Return the most recent match (last alphabetically, since date is in the name)
   178→		return matches[len(matches)-1]
   179→	}
   180→
   181→	// Fallback: construct a default path
   182→	return filepath.Join(sessionDir, agentSessionID+".json")
   183→}
   184→
   185→// GetSessionDir returns the directory where Gemini stores session transcripts.
   186→// Gemini stores sessions in ~/.gemini/tmp/<project-hash>/chats/
   187→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) GetSessionDir(repoPath string) (string, error) {
   188→	// Check for test environment override
   189→	if override := os.Getenv("ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR"); override != "" {
   190→		return override, nil
   191→	}
   192→
   193→	homeDir, err := os.UserHomeDir()
   194→	if err != nil {
   195→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to get home directory: %w", err)
   196→	}
   197→
   198→	// Gemini uses a hash of the project path for the directory name
   199→	projectDir := SanitizePathForGemini(repoPath)
   200→	return filepath.Join(homeDir, ".gemini", "tmp", projectDir, "chats"), nil
   201→}
   202→
   203→// ReadSession reads a session from Gemini's storage (JSON transcript file).
   204→// The session data is stored in NativeData as raw JSON bytes.
   205→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) ReadSession(input *agent.HookInput) (*agent.AgentSession, error) {
   206→	if input.SessionRef == "" {
   207→		return nil, errors.New("session reference (transcript path) is required")
   208→	}
   209→
   210→	// Read the raw JSON file
   211→	data, err := os.ReadFile(input.SessionRef)
   212→	if err != nil {
   213→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read transcript: %w", err)
   214→	}
   215→
   216→	// Parse to extract computed fields
   217→	modifiedFiles, err := ExtractModifiedFiles(data)
   218→	if err != nil {
   219→		// Non-fatal: we can still return the session without modified files
   220→		modifiedFiles = nil
   221→	}
   222→
   223→	return &agent.AgentSession{
   224→		SessionID:     input.SessionID,
   225→		AgentName:     g.Name(),
   226→		SessionRef:    input.SessionRef,
   227→		StartTime:     time.Now(),
   228→		NativeData:    data,
   229→		ModifiedFiles: modifiedFiles,
   230→	}, nil
   231→}
   232→
   233→// WriteSession writes a session to Gemini's storage (JSON transcript file).
   234→// Uses the NativeData field which contains raw JSON bytes.
   235→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) WriteSession(session *agent.AgentSession) error {
   236→	if session == nil {
   237→		return errors.New("session is nil")
   238→	}
   239→
   240→	// Verify this session belongs to Gemini CLI
   241→	if session.AgentName != "" && session.AgentName != g.Name() {
   242→		return fmt.Errorf("session belongs to agent %q, not %q", session.AgentName, g.Name())
   243→	}
   244→
   245→	if session.SessionRef == "" {
   246→		return errors.New("session reference (transcript path) is required")
   247→	}
   248→
   249→	if len(session.NativeData) == 0 {
   250→		return errors.New("session has no native data to write")
   251→	}
   252→
   253→	// Write the raw JSON data
   254→	if err := os.WriteFile(session.SessionRef, session.NativeData, 0o600); err != nil {
   255→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to write transcript: %w", err)
   256→	}
   257→
   258→	return nil
   259→}
   260→
   261→// FormatResumeCommand returns the command to resume a Gemini CLI session.
   262→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) FormatResumeCommand(sessionID string) string {
   263→	return "gemini --resume " + sessionID
   264→}
   265→
   266→// SanitizePathForGemini converts a path to Gemini's project directory format.
   267→// Gemini uses a hash-like sanitization similar to Claude.
   268→var nonAlphanumericRegex = regexp.MustCompile(`[^a-zA-Z0-9]`)
   269→
   270→func SanitizePathForGemini(path string) string {
   271→	return nonAlphanumericRegex.ReplaceAllString(path, "-")
   272→}
   273→
   274→// TranscriptAnalyzer interface implementation
   275→
   276→// GetTranscriptPosition returns the current message count of a Gemini transcript.
   277→// Gemini uses JSON format with a messages array, so position is the message count.
   278→// Returns 0 if the file doesn't exist or is empty.
   279→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) GetTranscriptPosition(path string) (int, error) {
   280→	if path == "" {
   281→		return 0, nil
   282→	}
   283→
   284→	data, err := os.ReadFile(path) //nolint:gosec // Reading from controlled transcript path
   285→	if err != nil {
   286→		if os.IsNotExist(err) {
   287→			return 0, nil
   288→		}
   289→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to read transcript: %w", err)
   290→	}
   291→
   292→	if len(data) == 0 {
   293→		return 0, nil
   294→	}
   295→
   296→	transcript, err := ParseTranscript(data)
   297→	if err != nil {
   298→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse transcript: %w", err)
   299→	}
   300→
   301→	return len(transcript.Messages), nil
   302→}
   303→
   304→// ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset extracts files modified since a given message index.
   305→// For Gemini (JSON format), offset is the starting message index.
   306→// Returns:
   307→//   - files: list of file paths modified by Gemini (from Write/Edit tools)
   308→//   - currentPosition: total number of messages in the transcript
   309→//   - error: any error encountered during reading
   310→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(path string, startOffset int) (files []string, currentPosition int, err error) {
   311→	if path == "" {
   312→		return nil, 0, nil
   313→	}
   314→
   315→	data, readErr := os.ReadFile(path) //nolint:gosec // Reading from controlled transcript path
   316→	if readErr != nil {
   317→		if os.IsNotExist(readErr) {
   318→			return nil, 0, nil
   319→		}
   320→		return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to read transcript: %w", readErr)
   321→	}
   322→
   323→	if len(data) == 0 {
   324→		return nil, 0, nil
   325→	}
   326→
   327→	transcript, parseErr := ParseTranscript(data)
   328→	if parseErr != nil {
   329→		return nil, 0, parseErr
   330→	}
   331→
   332→	totalMessages := len(transcript.Messages)
   333→
   334→	// Extract files from messages starting at startOffset
   335→	fileSet := make(map[string]bool)
   336→	for i := startOffset; i < len(transcript.Messages); i++ {
   337→		msg := transcript.Messages[i]
   338→		// Only process gemini messages (assistant messages)
   339→		if msg.Type != MessageTypeGemini {
   340→			continue
   341→		}
   342→
   343→		// Process tool calls in this message
   344→		for _, toolCall := range msg.ToolCalls {
   345→			// Check if it's a file modification tool
   346→			isModifyTool := false
   347→			for _, name := range FileModificationTools {
   348→				if toolCall.Name == name {
   349→					isModifyTool = true
   350→					break
   351→				}
   352→			}
   353→
   354→			if !isModifyTool {
   355→				continue
   356→			}
   357→
   358→			// Extract file path from args map
   359→			var file string
   360→			if fp, ok := toolCall.Args["file_path"].(string); ok && fp != "" {
   361→				file = fp
   362→			} else if p, ok := toolCall.Args["path"].(string); ok && p != "" {
   363→				file = p
   364→			} else if fn, ok := toolCall.Args["filename"].(string); ok && fn != "" {
   365→				file = fn
   366→			}
   367→
   368→			if file != "" && !fileSet[file] {
   369→				fileSet[file] = true
   370→				files = append(files, file)
   371→			}
   372→		}
   373→	}
   374→
   375→	return files, totalMessages, nil
   376→}
   377→
   378→// ChunkTranscript splits a Gemini JSON transcript by distributing messages across chunks.
   379→// Gemini uses JSON format with a {"messages": [...]} structure, so chunking splits
   380→// the messages array while preserving the JSON structure in each chunk.
   381→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) ChunkTranscript(content []byte, maxSize int) ([][]byte, error) {
   382→	var transcript GeminiTranscript
   383→	if err := json.Unmarshal(content, &transcript); err != nil {
   384→		// Fall back to JSONL chunking if not valid Gemini JSON
   385→		chunks, chunkErr := agent.ChunkJSONL(content, maxSize)
   386→		if chunkErr != nil {
   387→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to chunk as JSONL: %w", chunkErr)
   388→		}
   389→		return chunks, nil
   390→	}
   391→
   392→	if len(transcript.Messages) == 0 {
   393→		return [][]byte{content}, nil
   394→	}
   395→
   396→	var chunks [][]byte
   397→	var currentMessages []GeminiMessage
   398→	currentSize := len(`{"messages":[]}`) // Base JSON structure size
   399→
   400→	for i, msg := range transcript.Messages {
   401→		// Marshal message to get its size
   402→		msgBytes, err := json.Marshal(msg)
   403→		if err != nil {
   404→			logging.Warn(context.Background(), "failed to marshal Gemini message during chunking",
   405→				slog.Int("message_index", i),
   406→				slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   407→			)
   408→			continue
   409→		}
   410→		msgSize := len(msgBytes) + 1 // +1 for comma separator
   411→
   412→		if currentSize+msgSize > maxSize && len(currentMessages) > 0 {
   413→			// Save current chunk
   414→			chunkData, err := json.Marshal(GeminiTranscript{Messages: currentMessages})
   415→			if err != nil {
   416→				return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal chunk: %w", err)
   417→			}
   418→			chunks = append(chunks, chunkData)
   419→
   420→			// Start new chunk
   421→			currentMessages = nil
   422→			currentSize = len(`{"messages":[]}`)
   423→		}
   424→
   425→		currentMessages = append(currentMessages, msg)
   426→		currentSize += msgSize
   427→	}
   428→
   429→	// Add the last chunk
   430→	if len(currentMessages) > 0 {
   431→		chunkData, err := json.Marshal(GeminiTranscript{Messages: currentMessages})
   432→		if err != nil {
   433→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal final chunk: %w", err)
   434→		}
   435→		chunks = append(chunks, chunkData)
   436→	}
   437→
   438→	// Ensure we created at least one chunk (could be empty if all messages failed to marshal)
   439→	if len(chunks) == 0 {
   440→		return nil, errors.New("failed to create any chunks: all messages failed to marshal")
   441→	}
   442→
   443→	return chunks, nil
   444→}
   445→
   446→// ReassembleTranscript merges Gemini JSON chunks by combining their message arrays.
   447→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) ReassembleTranscript(chunks [][]byte) ([]byte, error) {
   448→	var allMessages []GeminiMessage
   449→
   450→	for _, chunk := range chunks {
   451→		var transcript GeminiTranscript
   452→		if err := json.Unmarshal(chunk, &transcript); err != nil {
   453→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to unmarshal chunk: %w", err)
   454→		}
   455→		allMessages = append(allMessages, transcript.Messages...)
   456→	}
   457→
   458→	result, err := json.Marshal(GeminiTranscript{Messages: allMessages})
   459→	if err != nil {
   460→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal reassembled transcript: %w", err)
   461→	}
   462→	return result, nil
   463→}
   464→
```

```
    11→
    12→// TestE2E_BasicWorkflow tests the fundamental workflow:
    13→// Agent creates a file -> User commits -> Checkpoint is created
    14→func TestE2E_BasicWorkflow(t *testing.T) {
    15→	t.Parallel()
    16→
    17→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    18→
    19→	// 1. Agent creates a file
    20→	t.Log("Step 1: Running agent to create hello.go")
    21→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)
    22→	require.NoError(t, err, "Agent should succeed")
    23→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    24→	t.Logf("Agent completed in %v", result.Duration)
    25→
    26→	// 2. Verify file was created with expected content
    27→	t.Log("Step 2: Verifying file was created")
    28→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("hello.go"), "hello.go should exist")
    29→	AssertHelloWorldProgram(t, env, "hello.go")
    30→
    31→	// 3. Verify rewind points exist (session should have created checkpoints)
    32→	t.Log("Step 3: Checking for rewind points")
    33→	points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    34→	assert.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(points), 1, "Should have at least 1 rewind point")
    35→	if len(points) > 0 {
    36→		t.Logf("Found %d rewind point(s), first: %s", len(points), points[0].Message)
    37→	}
    38→
    39→	// 4. User commits the changes with hooks
    40→	t.Log("Step 4: Committing changes with hooks")
    41→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Add hello world program", "hello.go")
    42→
    43→	// 5. Verify checkpoint was created (trailer in commit)
    44→	t.Log("Step 5: Verifying checkpoint")
    45→	checkpointID, err := env.GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory()
    46→	require.NoError(t, err, "Should find checkpoint in commit history")
    47→	assert.NotEmpty(t, checkpointID, "Commit should have Entire-Checkpoint trailer")
    48→	t.Logf("Checkpoint ID: %s", checkpointID)
    49→
    50→	// 6. Verify metadata branch exists
    51→	t.Log("Step 6: Checking metadata branch")
    52→	assert.True(t, env.BranchExists("entire/checkpoints/v1"),
    53→		"entire/checkpoints/v1 branch should exist")
    54→}
    55→
    56→// TestE2E_MultipleChanges tests multiple agent changes before commit.
    57→func TestE2E_MultipleChanges(t *testing.T) {
    58→	t.Parallel()
    59→
    60→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    61→
    62→	// 1. First agent action: create hello.go
    63→	t.Log("Step 1: Creating first file")
    64→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)
    65→	require.NoError(t, err)
    66→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    67→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("hello.go"))
    68→
    69→	// 2. Second agent action: create calc.go
    70→	t.Log("Step 2: Creating second file")
    71→	result, err = env.RunAgent(PromptCreateCalculator.Prompt)
    72→	require.NoError(t, err)
    73→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    74→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("calc.go"))
    75→
    76→	// 3. Verify multiple rewind points exist
    77→	t.Log("Step 3: Checking rewind points")
    78→	points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    79→	assert.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(points), 2, "Should have at least 2 rewind points")
    80→
```

> AGENT

Found it. The issue is clear now. The Gemini CLI has these approval modes:
- `default` - prompt for approval
- `auto_edit` - auto-approve edit tools only
- `yolo` - auto-approve all tools
- `plan` - read-only mode

The current code uses `--approval-mode auto_edit` which only auto-approves edit tools. When Gemini tries to use shell tools or other operations, it hangs waiting for user confirmation.

**Solution:** Change to `--approval-mode yolo` or use the `-y` flag for full automation in E2E tests.

You can test this manually with:

```bash
gemini -m gemini-2.5-flash -p 'Create a file called hello.go with a simple Go program that prints "Hello, World!". Use package main with a main function.' --approval-mode yolo
```

Or equivalently:

```bash
gemini -m gemini-2.5-flash -p 'Create a file called hello.go with a simple Go program that prints "Hello, World!". Use package main with a main function.' -y
```

Would you like me to fix the `GeminiCLIRunner` to use `--approval-mode yolo` instead of `auto_edit`?

but why is --allowed-tools not working?

> AGENT

Good question. Looking at the help output:

```
--allowed-tools             Tools that are allowed to run without confirmation  [array]
```

It expects an **array** (multiple arguments), but the current code passes a **comma-separated string**:

```go
// Current code (WRONG):
args = append(args, "--allowed-tools", strings.Join(allTools, ","))
// Results in: --allowed-tools "ShellTool(git status),ShellTool(git add),..."
```

Compare to how Claude Code does it correctly:

```go
// Claude (CORRECT):
// Claude CLI expects each tool as a separate argument after --allowedTools
args = append(args, "--allowedTools")
args = append(args, tools...)
// Results in: --allowedTools Edit Read Bash
```

For Gemini, it should be:

```go
// Should be:
for _, tool := range allTools {
    args = append(args, "--allowed-tools", tool)
}
// Results in: --allowed-tools "ShellTool(git status)" --allowed-tools "ShellTool(git add)" ...
```

Or possibly just:
```go
args = append(args, "--allowed-tools")
args = append(args, allTools...)
```

Want me to fix this in `agent_runner.go`?


Or try the space-separated array style:

```bash
gemini -m gemini-2.5-flash -p 'Create a file called hello.go with a simple Go program that prints "Hello, World!". Use package main with a main function.' --allowed-tools "ShellTool(git status)" "ShellTool(git add)" "ShellTool(git commit)" "ShellTool(git diff)" "ShellTool(git log)"
```

```bash
gemini -m gemini-2.5-flash -p 'Create a file called hello.go with a simple Go program that prints "Hello, World!". Use package main.' --approval-mode auto_edit --allowed-tools "ShellTool(git status),ShellTool(git add),ShellTool(git commit),ShellTool(git diff),ShellTool(git log)"
```

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

The command works manually but hangs in tests. The difference is likely **stdin/TTY handling**. When you run manually, you have a terminal. When the test runs it via `exec.Command`, there's no TTY and stdin might be in a weird state.

Looking at the code:

```go
cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "gemini", args...)
cmd.Dir = workDir

var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
cmd.Stdout = &stdout
cmd.Stderr = &stderr
// stdin is not set - inherits from parent process
```

Gemini might be waiting for stdin. Try explicitly closing it by setting stdin to `/dev/null`:

```go
cmd.Stdin = nil
```

Or more explicitly:

```go
devNull, _ := os.Open(os.DevNull)
defer devNull.Close()
cmd.Stdin = devNull
```

Want me to add this fix to `agent_runner.go`?

> AGENT

I see the issue. Step 2 calls:

```go
result, err = env.RunAgentWithTools(commitPrompt, []string{"Bash"})
```

But "Bash" is a Claude tool name. Gemini uses "ShellTool". So when "Bash" is appended to the allowed tools, Gemini doesn't recognize it.

The git commands (`git add`, `git commit`) are already in the default allowed tools, so theoretically it should work. But maybe Gemini is trying to use a generic shell approach that isn't whitelisted.

The fix would be to translate Claude tool names to Gemini equivalents. Let me check the `RunAgentWithTools` implementation in the test env:

```
    24→// defaultAgent holds the agent to test with, determined in TestMain.
    25→var defaultAgent string

    27→// testBinaryPath holds the path to the CLI binary built once in TestMain.
    28→// All tests share this binary to avoid repeated builds.

    30→
    31→// TestEnv manages an isolated test environment for E2E tests with real agent calls.
    32→type TestEnv struct {
    33→	T       *testing.T
    34→	RepoDir string
    35→	Agent   AgentRunner
    36→}
    37→
    38→// getTestBinary returns the path to the shared test binary.
    39→// It panics if TestMain hasn't run (testBinaryPath is empty).
    40→func getTestBinary() string {
    41→	if testBinaryPath == "" {
    42→		panic("testBinaryPath not set - TestMain must run before tests")
    43→	}
    44→	return testBinaryPath
    45→}
    46→
    47→// NewTestEnv creates a new isolated E2E test environment.
    48→func NewTestEnv(t *testing.T) *TestEnv {
    49→	t.Helper()
    50→
    51→	// Resolve symlinks on macOS where /var -> /private/var
    52→	repoDir := t.TempDir()
    53→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(repoDir); err == nil {
    54→		repoDir = resolved
    55→	}
    56→
    57→	// Create agent runner
    58→	agent := NewAgentRunner(defaultAgent, AgentRunnerConfig{})
    59→
    60→	return &TestEnv{
    61→		T:       t,
    62→		RepoDir: repoDir,
    63→		Agent:   agent,
    64→	}
    65→}
    66→
    67→// NewFeatureBranchEnv creates an E2E test environment ready for testing.
    68→// It initializes the repo, creates an initial commit on main,
    69→// checks out a feature branch, and sets up agent hooks.
    70→func NewFeatureBranchEnv(t *testing.T, strategyName string) *TestEnv {
    71→	t.Helper()
    72→
    73→	env := NewTestEnv(t)
    74→	env.InitRepo()
    75→	env.WriteFile("README.md", "# Test Repository\n\nThis is a test repository for E2E testing.\n")
    76→	env.GitAdd("README.md")
    77→	env.GitCommit("Initial commit")
    78→	env.GitCheckoutNewBranch("feature/e2e-test")
    79→
    80→	// Use `entire enable` to set up everything (hooks, settings, etc.)
    81→	// This sets up .entire/settings.json and .claude/settings.json with hooks
    82→	env.RunEntireEnable(strategyName)
    83→
    84→	// Commit all files created by `entire enable` so they survive git stash -u operations.
    85→	// Without this, stash operations would stash away the hooks config and entire settings,
    86→	// causing hooks to not fire for subsequent prompts and stash pop conflicts.
    87→	// Using "git add ." is safe in test repos since we control all content.
    88→	env.GitAddAll()
    89→	env.GitCommit("Add entire and agent config")
    90→
    91→	return env
    92→}
    93→
    94→// RunEntireEnable runs `entire enable` to set up the project with hooks.

    99→	args := []string{

   412→// GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved stages and commits files, simulating what happens when
   413→// a user removes the Entire-Checkpoint trailer during commit message editing.
   414→// This tests the opt-out behavior where removing the trailer skips condensation.
   415→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved(message string, files ...string) {
   416→	env.T.Helper()
   417→
   418→	// Stage files using go-git
   419→	for _, file := range files {
   420→		env.GitAdd(file)
   421→	}
   422→
   423→	// Create a temp file for the commit message
   424→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
   425→	//nolint:gosec // test code, permissions are intentionally standard
   426→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
   427→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
   428→	}
   429→
   430→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook
   431→	//nolint:gosec,noctx // test code, args are from trusted test setup, no context needed
   432→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile)
   433→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   434→	prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   435→		"ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1",
   436→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".claude"),
   437→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".gemini"),
   438→	)
   439→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   440→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
   441→	}
   442→
   443→	// Read the modified message (with trailer added by hook)
   444→	//nolint:gosec // test code, path is from test setup
   445→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
   446→	if err != nil {
   447→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
   448→	}
   449→
   450→	// REMOVE the Entire-Checkpoint trailer (simulating user editing the message)
   451→	lines := strings.Split(string(modifiedMsg), "\n")
   452→	var cleanedLines []string
   453→	for _, line := range lines {
   454→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:") {
   455→			continue
   456→		}
   457→		if strings.Contains(line, "Remove the Entire-Checkpoint trailer") {
   458→			continue
   459→		}
   460→		if strings.Contains(line, "trailer will be added to your next commit") {
   461→			continue
   462→		}
   463→		cleanedLines = append(cleanedLines, line)
   464→	}
   465→	cleanedMsg := strings.TrimRight(strings.Join(cleanedLines, "\n"), "\n") + "\n"
   466→
   467→	// Create the commit using go-git with the cleaned message (no trailer)
   468→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   469→	if err != nil {
   470→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   471→	}
   472→
   473→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   474→	if err != nil {
   475→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   476→	}
   477→
   478→	_, err = worktree.Commit(cleanedMsg, &git.CommitOptions{
   479→		Author: &object.Signature{
   480→			Name:  "E2E Test User",
   481→			Email: "e2e-test@example.com",
   482→			When:  time.Now(),
   483→		},
   484→	})
   485→	if err != nil {
   486→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   487→	}
   488→
   489→	// Run post-commit hook - since trailer was removed, no condensation should happen
   490→	//nolint:gosec,noctx // test code, args are from trusted test setup, no context needed
   491→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
   492→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   493→	postCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   494→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".claude"),
   495→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".gemini"),
   496→	)
   497→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   498→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
   499→	}
   500→}

   531→// RewindPoint mirrors the rewind --list JSON output.
   532→type RewindPoint struct {
   533→	ID               string    `json:"id"`
   534→	Message          string    `json:"message"`
   535→	MetadataDir      string    `json:"metadata_dir"`
   536→	Date             time.Time `json:"date"`
   537→	IsTaskCheckpoint bool      `json:"is_task_checkpoint"`
   538→	ToolUseID        string    `json:"tool_use_id"`
   539→	IsLogsOnly       bool      `json:"is_logs_only"`
   540→	CondensationID   string    `json:"condensation_id"`
   541→}
   542→
   543→// GetRewindPoints returns available rewind points using the CLI.
   544→func (env *TestEnv) GetRewindPoints() []RewindPoint {
   545→	env.T.Helper()
   546→
   547→	//nolint:gosec,noctx // test code, args are static, no context needed
   548→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "rewind", "--list")
   549→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   550→
   551→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   552→	if err != nil {
   553→		env.T.Fatalf("rewind --list failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   554→	}
   555→
   556→	// Parse JSON output
   557→	var jsonPoints []struct {
   558→		ID               string `json:"id"`
   559→		Message          string `json:"message"`
   560→		MetadataDir      string `json:"metadata_dir"`
   561→		Date             string `json:"date"`
   562→		IsTaskCheckpoint bool   `json:"is_task_checkpoint"`
   563→		ToolUseID        string `json:"tool_use_id"`
   564→		IsLogsOnly       bool   `json:"is_logs_only"`
   565→		CondensationID   string `json:"condensation_id"`
   566→	}
   567→
   568→	if err := json.Unmarshal(output, &jsonPoints); err != nil {
   569→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to parse rewind points: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   570→	}
   571→
   572→	points := make([]RewindPoint, len(jsonPoints))
   573→	for i, jp := range jsonPoints {
   574→		//nolint:errcheck // date parsing failure is acceptable, defaults to zero time
   575→		date, _ := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, jp.Date)
   576→		points[i] = RewindPoint{
   577→			ID:               jp.ID,
   578→			Message:          jp.Message,
   579→			MetadataDir:      jp.MetadataDir,
   580→			Date:             date,
   581→			IsTaskCheckpoint: jp.IsTaskCheckpoint,
   582→			ToolUseID:        jp.ToolUseID,
   583→			IsLogsOnly:       jp.IsLogsOnly,
   584→			CondensationID:   jp.CondensationID,
   585→		}
   586→	}
   587→
   588→	return points
   589→}

   608→// BranchExists checks if a branch exists in the repository.
   609→func (env *TestEnv) BranchExists(branchName string) bool {
   610→	env.T.Helper()
   611→
   612→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   613→	if err != nil {
   614→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   615→	}
   616→
   617→	refs, err := repo.References()
   618→	if err != nil {
   619→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get references: %v", err)
   620→	}
   621→
   622→	found := false
   623→	//nolint:errcheck,gosec // ForEach callback doesn't return errors we need to handle
   624→	refs.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
   625→		if ref.Name().Short() == branchName {
   626→			found = true
   627→		}
   628→		return nil
   629→	})
   630→
   631→	return found
   632→}

   634→// ListBranchesWithPrefix returns all branches that start with the given prefix.
   635→func (env *TestEnv) ListBranchesWithPrefix(prefix string) []string {
   636→	env.T.Helper()
   637→
   638→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   639→	if err != nil {
   640→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   641→	}
   642→
   643→	refs, err := repo.References()
   644→	if err != nil {
   645→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get references: %v", err)
   646→	}
   647→
   648→	var branches []string
   649→	//nolint:errcheck,gosec // ForEach callback doesn't return errors we need to handle
   650→	refs.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
   651→		name := ref.Name().Short()
   652→		if strings.HasPrefix(name, prefix) {
   653→			branches = append(branches, name)
   654→		}
   655→		return nil
   656→	})
   657→
   658→	return branches
   659→}

   678→
   679→// GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory walks backwards from HEAD and returns
   680→// the checkpoint ID from the first commit with an Entire-Checkpoint trailer.
   681→// Returns an error if no checkpoint trailer is found in any commit.
   682→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory() (string, error) {
   683→	env.T.Helper()
   684→
   685→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   686→	if err != nil {
   687→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   688→	}
   689→
   690→	head, err := repo.Head()
   691→	if err != nil {
   692→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   693→	}
   694→
   695→	commitIter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: head.Hash()})
   696→	if err != nil {
   697→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to iterate commits: %v", err)
   698→	}
   699→
   700→	var checkpointID string
   701→	//nolint:errcheck,gosec // ForEach callback returns error to stop iteration, not a real error
   702→	commitIter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
   703→		// Look for Entire-Checkpoint trailer
   704→		for _, line := range strings.Split(c.Message, "\n") {
   705→			line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
   706→			if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:") {
   707→				checkpointID = strings.TrimSpace(strings.TrimPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:"))
   708→				return errors.New("stop iteration")
   709→			}
   710→		}
   711→		return nil
   712→	})
   713→
   714→	if checkpointID == "" {
   715→		return "", errors.New("no commit with Entire-Checkpoint trailer found in history")
   716→	}
   717→
   718→	return checkpointID, nil
   719→}
   720→
   721→// GetLatestCheckpointID returns the checkpoint ID from HEAD commit, or empty string if none.
   722→// This is a convenience wrapper that doesn't fail the test on missing checkpoint.
   723→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointID() string {
   724→	env.T.Helper()
   725→
   726→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   727→	if err != nil {
   728→		return ""
   729→	}
   730→
   731→	head, err := repo.Head()
   732→	if err != nil {
   733→		return ""
   734→	}
   735→
   736→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(head.Hash())
   737→	if err != nil {
   738→		return ""
   739→	}
   740→
   741→	// Look for Entire-Checkpoint trailer in HEAD commit only
   742→	for _, line := range strings.Split(commit.Message, "\n") {
   743→		line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
   744→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:") {
   745→			return strings.TrimSpace(strings.TrimPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:"))
   746→		}
   747→	}
   748→	return ""
   749→}
   750→
   751→// safeIDPrefix returns first 12 chars of ID or the full ID if shorter.
   752→// Use this when logging checkpoint IDs to avoid index out of bounds panic.
   753→func safeIDPrefix(id string) string {
   754→	if len(id) >= 12 {
   755→		return id[:12]
   756→	}
   757→	return id
   758→}

   760→// RunCLI runs the entire CLI with the given arguments and returns stdout.
   761→func (env *TestEnv) RunCLI(args ...string) string {
   762→	env.T.Helper()
   763→	output, err := env.RunCLIWithError(args...)
   764→	if err != nil {

   766→	}
   767→	return output

   769→
   770→// RunCLIWithError runs the entire CLI and returns output and error.
   771→func (env *TestEnv) RunCLIWithError(args ...string) (string, error) {
   772→	env.T.Helper()
   773→

   863→// GetCommitCount returns the number of commits on the current branch.
   864→func (env *TestEnv) GetCommitCount() int {
   865→	env.T.Helper()
   866→
   867→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   868→	if err != nil {
   869→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   870→	}
   871→
   872→	head, err := repo.Head()
   873→	if err != nil {
   874→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   875→	}
   876→
   877→	commitIter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: head.Hash()})
   878→	if err != nil {

   880→	}
   881→
   882→	count := 0
   883→	//nolint:errcheck,gosec // ForEach callback doesn't return errors we need to handle
   884→	commitIter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
   885→		count++
   886→		return nil
   887→	})
   888→
   889→	return count
   890→}

   892→// GetAllCheckpointIDsFromHistory walks backwards from HEAD and returns
   893→// all checkpoint IDs from commits with Entire-Checkpoint trailers.
   894→func (env *TestEnv) GetAllCheckpointIDsFromHistory() []string {
   895→	env.T.Helper()
   896→
   897→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   898→	if err != nil {
   899→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   900→	}
   901→
   902→	head, err := repo.Head()
   903→	if err != nil {
   904→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   905→	}
   906→
   907→	commitIter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: head.Hash()})
   908→	if err != nil {
   909→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to iterate commits: %v", err)
   910→	}
   911→
   912→	var checkpointIDs []string
   913→	//nolint:errcheck,gosec // ForEach callback doesn't return errors we need to handle
   914→	commitIter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
   915→		for _, line := range strings.Split(c.Message, "\n") {
   916→			line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
   917→			if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:") {
   918→				id := strings.TrimSpace(strings.TrimPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:"))
   919→				checkpointIDs = append(checkpointIDs, id)
   920→			}
   921→		}
   922→		return nil
   923→	})
   924→
   925→	return checkpointIDs
   926→}

   928→// ReadFileFromBranch reads a file's content from a specific branch's tree.
   929→// Returns the content and true if found, empty string and false if not found.
   930→func (env *TestEnv) ReadFileFromBranch(branchName, filePath string) (string, bool) {
   931→	env.T.Helper()
   932→
   933→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   934→	if err != nil {
   935→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   936→	}
   937→
   938→	// Get the branch reference
   939→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), true)
   940→	if err != nil {
   941→		// Try as a remote-style ref
   942→		ref, err = repo.Reference(plumbing.ReferenceName("refs/heads/"+branchName), true)
   943→		if err != nil {
   944→			return "", false
   945→		}
   946→	}
   947→
   948→	// Get the commit
   949→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   950→	if err != nil {
   951→		return "", false
   952→	}
   953→
   954→	// Get the tree
   955→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   956→	if err != nil {
   957→		return "", false
   958→	}
   959→
   960→	// Get the file
   961→	file, err := tree.File(filePath)
   962→	if err != nil {
   963→		return "", false
   964→	}
   965→
   966→	// Get the content
   967→	content, err := file.Contents()
   968→	if err != nil {
   969→		return "", false
   970→	}
   971→
   972→	return content, true
   973→}

   975→// CheckpointValidation contains expected values for checkpoint validation.
   976→type CheckpointValidation struct {
   977→	// CheckpointID is the expected checkpoint ID
   978→	CheckpointID string
   979→
   980→	// Strategy is the expected strategy name (optional)
   981→	Strategy string
   982→
   983→	// FilesTouched are the expected files in files_touched (optional)
   984→	FilesTouched []string
   985→
   986→	// ExpectedPrompts are strings that should appear in prompt.txt (optional)
   987→	ExpectedPrompts []string
   988→
   989→	// ExpectedTranscriptContent are strings that should appear in full.jsonl (optional)
   990→	ExpectedTranscriptContent []string
   991→}
   992→
   993→// ValidateCheckpoint performs comprehensive validation of a checkpoint on the metadata branch.
   994→func (env *TestEnv) ValidateCheckpoint(v CheckpointValidation) {
   995→	env.T.Helper()
   996→
   997→	metadataBranch := "entire/checkpoints/v1"
   998→
   999→	// Compute sharded path: <id[:2]>/<id[2:]>/
  1000→	if len(v.CheckpointID) < 3 {
  1001→		env.T.Fatalf("Checkpoint ID too short: %s", v.CheckpointID)
  1002→	}
  1003→	shardedPath := v.CheckpointID[:2] + "/" + v.CheckpointID[2:]
  1004→
  1005→	// Validate root metadata.json exists and has expected fields
  1006→	summaryPath := shardedPath + "/metadata.json"
  1007→	summaryContent, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(metadataBranch, summaryPath)
  1008→	if !found {
  1009→		env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary not found at %s", summaryPath)
  1010→	} else {
  1011→		var summary map[string]any
  1012→		if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(summaryContent), &summary); err != nil {
  1013→			env.T.Errorf("Failed to parse CheckpointSummary: %v", err)
  1014→		} else {
  1015→			// Validate checkpoint_id
  1016→			if cpID, ok := summary["checkpoint_id"].(string); !ok || cpID != v.CheckpointID {
  1017→				env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.checkpoint_id = %v, want %s", summary["checkpoint_id"], v.CheckpointID)
  1018→			}
  1019→			// Validate strategy if specified
  1020→			if v.Strategy != "" {
  1021→				if strategy, ok := summary["strategy"].(string); !ok || strategy != v.Strategy {
  1022→					env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.strategy = %v, want %s", summary["strategy"], v.Strategy)
  1023→				}
  1024→			}
  1025→			// Validate sessions array exists
  1026→			if sessions, ok := summary["sessions"].([]any); !ok || len(sessions) == 0 {
  1027→				env.T.Error("CheckpointSummary.sessions should have at least one entry")
  1028→			}
  1029→			// Validate files_touched if specified
  1030→			if len(v.FilesTouched) > 0 {
  1031→				if filesTouched, ok := summary["files_touched"].([]any); ok {
  1032→					touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
  1033→					for _, f := range filesTouched {
  1034→						if s, ok := f.(string); ok {
  1035→							touchedSet[s] = true
  1036→						}
  1037→					}
  1038→					for _, expected := range v.FilesTouched {
  1039→						if !touchedSet[expected] {
  1040→							env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.files_touched missing %q", expected)
  1041→						}
  1042→					}
  1043→				}
  1044→			}
  1045→		}
  1046→	}
  1047→
  1048→	// Validate session metadata.json exists
  1049→	sessionMetadataPath := shardedPath + "/0/metadata.json"
  1050→	sessionContent, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(metadataBranch, sessionMetadataPath)
  1051→	if !found {
  1052→		env.T.Errorf("Session metadata not found at %s", sessionMetadataPath)
  1053→	} else {
  1054→		var metadata map[string]any
  1055→		if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(sessionContent), &metadata); err != nil {
  1056→			env.T.Errorf("Failed to parse session metadata: %v", err)
  1057→		} else {
  1058→			// Validate checkpoint_id matches
  1059→			if cpID, ok := metadata["checkpoint_id"].(string); !ok || cpID != v.CheckpointID {
  1060→				env.T.Errorf("Session metadata.checkpoint_id = %v, want %s", metadata["checkpoint_id"], v.CheckpointID)
  1061→			}
  1062→			// Validate created_at exists
  1063→			if _, ok := metadata["created_at"].(string); !ok {
  1064→				env.T.Error("Session metadata.created_at should exist")
  1065→			}
  1066→		}
  1067→	}
  1068→
  1069→	// Validate transcript is valid JSONL (only enforce for Claude Code, log warnings for others)
  1070→	transcriptPath := shardedPath + "/0/full.jsonl"
  1071→	transcriptContent, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(metadataBranch, transcriptPath)
  1072→	if !found {
  1073→		env.T.Errorf("Transcript not found at %s", transcriptPath)
  1074→	} else {
  1075→		// Check each line is valid JSON
  1076→		// Only enforce JSONL format for Claude Code - other agents may have different formats
  1077→		isClaudeCode := env.Agent.Name() == AgentNameClaudeCode
  1078→		lines := strings.Split(transcriptContent, "\n")
  1079→		validLines := 0
  1080→		for i, line := range lines {
  1081→			line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
  1082→			if line == "" {
  1083→				continue
  1084→			}
  1085→			validLines++
  1086→			var obj map[string]any
  1087→			if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(line), &obj); err != nil {
  1088→				if isClaudeCode {
  1089→					env.T.Errorf("Transcript line %d is not valid JSON: %v", i+1, err)
  1090→				}
  1091→				// For non-Claude agents, just log (transcript format may differ)
  1092→			}
  1093→		}
  1094→		if validLines == 0 {
  1095→			env.T.Error("Transcript is empty (no valid JSONL lines)")
  1096→		}
  1097→		// Check expected content
  1098→		for _, expected := range v.ExpectedTranscriptContent {
  1099→			if !strings.Contains(transcriptContent, expected) {
  1100→				env.T.Errorf("Transcript should contain %q", expected)
  1101→			}
  1102→		}
  1103→	}
  1104→
  1105→	// Validate prompt.txt contains expected prompts
  1106→	if len(v.ExpectedPrompts) > 0 {
  1107→		promptPath := shardedPath + "/0/prompt.txt"
  1108→		promptContent, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(metadataBranch, promptPath)
  1109→		if !found {
  1110→			env.T.Errorf("Prompt file not found at %s", promptPath)
  1111→		} else {
  1112→			for _, expected := range v.ExpectedPrompts {
  1113→				if !strings.Contains(promptContent, expected) {
  1114→					env.T.Errorf("Prompt file should contain %q", expected)
  1115→				}
  1116→			}
  1117→		}
  1118→	}
  1119→
  1120→	// Validate content hash exists and matches transcript
  1121→	hashPath := shardedPath + "/0/content_hash.txt"
  1122→	hashContent, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(metadataBranch, hashPath)
  1123→	if !found {
  1124→		env.T.Errorf("Content hash not found at %s", hashPath)
  1125→	} else if transcriptContent != "" {
  1126→		// Verify hash matches
  1127→		hash := sha256.Sum256([]byte(transcriptContent))
  1128→		expectedHash := "sha256:" + hex.EncodeToString(hash[:])
  1129→		storedHash := strings.TrimSpace(hashContent)
  1130→		if storedHash != expectedHash {
  1131→			env.T.Errorf("Content hash mismatch:\n  stored:   %s\n  expected: %s", storedHash, expectedHash)
  1132→		}
  1133→	}
  1134→}
  1135→
```

> AGENT

I see the issue now. The allowed tools list has:

```
ShellTool(git status)
ShellTool(git add)
ShellTool(git commit)
```

But Gemini might need the **full command** to be whitelisted, not just the prefix. So `git add hello.go` might not match `ShellTool(git add)`.

Can you test if Gemini supports wildcards? Try this command manually:

```bash
gemini -m gemini-2.5-flash -p 'Run: git add hello.go && git commit -m "test"' --approval-mode auto_edit --allowed-tools "ShellTool(git *)"
```

Or check if it needs the full commands:

```bash
gemini -m gemini-2.5-flash -p 'Run: git status' --approval-mode auto_edit --allowed-tools "ShellTool(git status)"
```

vs

```bash
gemini -m gemini-2.5-flash -p 'Run: git add hello.go' --approval-mode auto_edit --allowed-tools "ShellTool(git add)"
```

This will tell us if the matching is exact or prefix-based.

So wildcards don't work - `ShellTool(git *)` was denied. Gemini requires exact command matching or a specific pattern format that we don't know.

The simplest solution for E2E tests is to use `--approval-mode yolo` which auto-approves everything. Since tests run in isolated temp directories, this is safe:

```json
  "new_string": "func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error) {\n\t// Build command: gemini -m <model> -p \"<prompt>\" --approval-mode yolo\n\t// --approval-mode yolo: auto-approves all tools (safe for E2E tests in isolated temp dirs)\n\t// Note: We use yolo mode because Gemini's --allowed-tools requires exact command matching\n\t// (e.g., \"ShellTool(git add)\" won't match \"git add hello.go\"), making it impractical for tests.\n\t_ = tools // tools parameter ignored - yolo mode approves everything\n\targs := []string{\n\t\t\"-m\", r.Model,\n\t\t\"-p\", prompt,\n\t\t\"--approval-mode\", \"yolo\",\n\t}"
}
```

The git commands worked. The error was for an `echo` command Gemini tried to run afterwards to confirm completion - that's what's causing the hang (retrying denied commands).

So the fix is either:
1. Add more commands to allowed list (but Gemini might try other commands too)
2. Use `--approval-mode yolo` for E2E tests (simplest)

```
    25→// newGitHookContext creates a new git hook context with logging initialized.
    26→func newGitHookContext(hookName string) *gitHookContext {
    27→	g := &gitHookContext{

    31→		strategyName: unknownStrategyName,

    38→// logInvoked logs that the hook was invoked.
    39→func (g *gitHookContext) logInvoked(extraAttrs ...any) {
    40→	attrs := []any{
    41→		slog.String("hook", g.hookName),
    42→		slog.String("hook_type", "git"),
    43→		slog.String("strategy", g.strategyName),
    44→	}
    45→	logging.Debug(g.ctx, g.hookName+" hook invoked", append(attrs, extraAttrs...)...)
    46→}
    47→
    48→// logCompleted logs hook completion with duration at DEBUG level.
    49→// The actual work logging (checkpoint operations) happens at INFO level in the handlers.
    50→func (g *gitHookContext) logCompleted(err error, extraAttrs ...any) {
    51→	attrs := []any{

    53→		slog.String("hook_type", "git"),
    54→		slog.String("strategy", g.strategyName),
    55→		slog.Bool("success", err == nil),

    57→	logging.LogDuration(g.ctx, slog.LevelDebug, g.hookName+" hook completed", g.start, append(attrs, extraAttrs...)...)

    72→	return logging.Close
```

```
    30→
    31→// hasTTY checks if /dev/tty is available for interactive prompts.
    32→// Returns false when running as an agent subprocess (no controlling terminal).
    33→//
    34→// In test environments, ENTIRE_TEST_TTY overrides the real check:
    35→//   - ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 → simulate human (TTY available)
    36→//   - ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=0 → simulate agent (no TTY)
    37→func hasTTY() bool {
    38→	if v := os.Getenv("ENTIRE_TEST_TTY"); v != "" {
    39→		return v == "1"
    40→	}
    41→	tty, err := os.OpenFile("/dev/tty", os.O_RDWR, 0)
    42→	if err != nil {
    43→		return false
    44→	}
    45→	_ = tty.Close()
    46→	return true
    47→}
    48→
    49→// askConfirmTTY prompts the user for a yes/no confirmation via /dev/tty.
    50→// This works even when stdin is redirected (e.g., git commit -m).
    51→// Returns true for yes, false for no. If TTY is unavailable, returns the default.
    52→// If context is non-empty, it is displayed on a separate line before the prompt.
    53→func askConfirmTTY(prompt string, context string, defaultYes bool) bool {
    54→	// In test mode, don't try to interact with the real TTY — just use the default.
    55→	// ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 simulates "a human is present" for the hasTTY() check
    56→	// but we can't actually read from the TTY in tests.
    57→	if os.Getenv("ENTIRE_TEST_TTY") != "" {
    58→		return defaultYes
    59→	}
    60→
    61→	// Open /dev/tty for both reading and writing
    62→	// This is the controlling terminal, which works even when stdin/stderr are redirected
    63→	tty, err := os.OpenFile("/dev/tty", os.O_RDWR, 0)
    64→	if err != nil {
    65→		// Can't open TTY (e.g., running in CI), use default
    66→		return defaultYes
    67→	}
    68→	defer tty.Close()
    69→
    70→	// Show context if provided
    71→	if context != "" {
    72→		fmt.Fprintf(tty, "%s\n", context)
    73→	}
    74→
    75→	// Show prompt with default indicator
    76→	// Write to tty directly, not stderr, since git hooks may redirect stderr to /dev/null
    77→	var hint string
    78→	if defaultYes {
    79→		hint = "[Y/n]"
    80→	} else {
    81→		hint = "[y/N]"
    82→	}
    83→	fmt.Fprintf(tty, "%s %s ", prompt, hint)
    84→
    85→	// Read response
    86→	reader := bufio.NewReader(tty)
    87→	response, err := reader.ReadString('\n')
    88→	if err != nil {
    89→		return defaultYes
    90→	}
    91→
    92→	response = strings.TrimSpace(strings.ToLower(response))
    93→	switch response {
    94→	case "y", "yes":
    95→		return true
    96→	case "n", "no":
    97→		return false
    98→	default:
    99→		// Empty or invalid input - use default
   100→		return defaultYes
   101→	}
   102→}
   103→
   104→// CommitMsg is called by the git commit-msg hook after the user edits the message.
   105→// If the message contains only our trailer (no actual user content), strip it
   106→// so git will abort the commit due to empty message.
   107→//
   108→//nolint:unparam // error return required by interface but hooks must return nil
   109→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) CommitMsg(commitMsgFile string) error {
   110→	content, err := os.ReadFile(commitMsgFile) //nolint:gosec // Path comes from git hook
   111→	if err != nil {
   112→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   113→	}
   114→
   115→	message := string(content)
   116→
   117→	// Check if our trailer is present (ParseCheckpoint validates format, so found==true means valid)
   118→	if _, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(message); !found {
   119→		// No trailer, nothing to do
   120→		return nil
   121→	}
   122→
   123→	// Check if there's any user content (non-comment, non-trailer lines)
   124→	if !hasUserContent(message) {
   125→		// No user content - strip the trailer so git aborts
   126→		message = stripCheckpointTrailer(message)
   127→		if err := os.WriteFile(commitMsgFile, []byte(message), 0o600); err != nil {
   128→			return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   129→		}
   130→	}
   131→
   132→	return nil
   133→}
   134→
   135→// hasUserContent checks if the message has any content besides comments and our trailer.
   136→func hasUserContent(message string) bool {
   137→	trailerPrefix := trailers.CheckpointTrailerKey + ":"
   138→	for _, line := range strings.Split(message, "\n") {
   139→		trimmed := strings.TrimSpace(line)
   140→		// Skip empty lines
   141→		if trimmed == "" {
   142→			continue
   143→		}
   144→		// Skip comment lines
   145→		if strings.HasPrefix(trimmed, "#") {
   146→			continue
   147→		}
   148→		// Skip our trailer line
   149→		if strings.HasPrefix(trimmed, trailerPrefix) {
   150→			continue
   151→		}
   152→		// Found user content
   153→		return true
   154→	}
   155→	return false
   156→}
   157→
   158→// stripCheckpointTrailer removes the Entire-Checkpoint trailer line from the message.
   159→func stripCheckpointTrailer(message string) string {
   160→	trailerPrefix := trailers.CheckpointTrailerKey + ":"
   161→	var result []string
   162→	for _, line := range strings.Split(message, "\n") {
   163→		if !strings.HasPrefix(strings.TrimSpace(line), trailerPrefix) {
   164→			result = append(result, line)
   165→		}
   166→	}
   167→	return strings.Join(result, "\n")
   168→}
   169→
   170→// isGitSequenceOperation checks if git is currently in the middle of a rebase,
   171→// cherry-pick, or revert operation. During these operations, commits are being
   172→// replayed and should not be linked to agent sessions.
   173→//
   174→// Detects:
   175→//   - rebase: .git/rebase-merge/ or .git/rebase-apply/ directories
   176→//   - cherry-pick: .git/CHERRY_PICK_HEAD file
   177→//   - revert: .git/REVERT_HEAD file
   178→func isGitSequenceOperation() bool {
   179→	// Get git directory (handles worktrees and relative paths correctly)
   180→	gitDir, err := GetGitDir()
   181→	if err != nil {
   182→		return false // Can't determine, assume not in sequence operation
   183→	}
   184→
   185→	// Check for rebase state directories
   186→	if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(gitDir, "rebase-merge")); err == nil {
   187→		return true
   188→	}
   189→	if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(gitDir, "rebase-apply")); err == nil {
   190→		return true
   191→	}
   192→
   193→	// Check for cherry-pick and revert state files
   194→	if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(gitDir, "CHERRY_PICK_HEAD")); err == nil {
   195→		return true
   196→	}
   197→	if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(gitDir, "REVERT_HEAD")); err == nil {
   198→		return true
   199→	}
   200→
   201→	return false
   202→}
   203→
   204→// PrepareCommitMsg is called by the git prepare-commit-msg hook.
   205→// Adds an Entire-Checkpoint trailer to the commit message with a stable checkpoint ID.
   206→// Only adds a trailer if there's actually new session content to condense.
   207→// The actual condensation happens in PostCommit - if the user removes the trailer,
   208→// the commit will not be linked to the session (useful for "manual" commits).
   209→// For amended commits, preserves the existing checkpoint ID.
   210→//
   211→// The source parameter indicates how the commit was initiated:
   212→//   - "" or "template": normal editor flow - adds trailer with explanatory comment
   213→//   - "message": using -m or -F flag - prompts user interactively via /dev/tty
   214→//   - "merge", "squash": skip trailer entirely (auto-generated messages)
   215→//   - "commit": amend operation - preserves existing trailer or restores from LastCheckpointID
   216→//
   217→
   218→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) PrepareCommitMsg(commitMsgFile string, source string) error {
   219→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   220→
   221→	// Skip during rebase, cherry-pick, or revert operations
   222→	// These are replaying existing commits and should not be linked to agent sessions
   223→	if isGitSequenceOperation() {
   224→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: skipped during git sequence operation",
   225→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   226→			slog.String("source", source),
   227→		)
   228→		return nil
   229→	}
   230→
   231→	// Skip for merge and squash sources
   232→	// These are auto-generated messages - not from Claude sessions
   233→	switch source {
   234→	case "merge", "squash":
   235→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: skipped for source",
   236→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   237→			slog.String("source", source),
   238→		)
   239→		return nil
   240→	}
   241→
   242→	// Handle amend (source="commit") separately: preserve or restore trailer
   243→	if source == "commit" {
   244→		return s.handleAmendCommitMsg(logCtx, commitMsgFile)
   245→	}
   246→
   247→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
   248→	if err != nil {
   249→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   250→	}
   251→
   252→	worktreePath, err := GetWorktreePath()
   253→	if err != nil {
   254→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   255→	}
   256→
   257→	// Find all active sessions for this worktree
   258→	// We match by worktree (not BaseCommit) because the user may have made
   259→	// intermediate commits without entering new prompts, causing HEAD to diverge
   260→	sessions, err := s.findSessionsForWorktree(worktreePath)
   261→	if err != nil || len(sessions) == 0 {
   262→		// No active sessions or error listing - silently skip (hooks must be resilient)
   263→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: no active sessions",
   264→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   265→			slog.String("source", source),
   266→		)
   267→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Intentional: hooks must be silent on failure
   268→	}
   269→
   270→	// Fast path: when an agent is committing (ACTIVE session + no TTY), skip
   271→	// content detection and interactive prompts. The agent can't respond to TTY
   272→	// prompts and the content detection can miss mid-session work (no shadow
   273→	// branch yet, transcript analysis may fail). Generate a checkpoint ID and
   274→	// add the trailer directly.
   275→	if !hasTTY() {
   276→		for _, state := range sessions {
   277→			if state.Phase.IsActive() {
   278→				return s.addTrailerForAgentCommit(logCtx, commitMsgFile, state, source)
   279→			}
   280→		}
   281→	}
   282→
   283→	// Check if any session has new content to condense
   284→	sessionsWithContent := s.filterSessionsWithNewContent(repo, sessions)
   285→
   286→	if len(sessionsWithContent) == 0 {
   287→		// No new content — no trailer needed
   288→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: no content to link",
   289→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   290→			slog.String("source", source),
   291→			slog.Int("sessions_found", len(sessions)),
   292→		)
   293→		return nil
   294→	}
   295→
   296→	// Read current commit message
   297→	content, err := os.ReadFile(commitMsgFile) //nolint:gosec // commitMsgFile is provided by git hook
   298→	if err != nil {
   299→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   300→	}
   301→
   302→	message := string(content)
   303→
   304→	// Check if trailer already exists (ParseCheckpoint validates format, so found==true means valid)
   305→	if existingCpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(message); found {
   306→		// Trailer already exists (e.g., amend) - keep it
   307→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: trailer already exists",
   308→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   309→			slog.String("source", source),
   310→			slog.String("existing_checkpoint_id", existingCpID.String()),
   311→		)
   312→		return nil
   313→	}
   314→
   315→	// Generate a fresh checkpoint ID
   316→	checkpointID, err := id.Generate()
   317→	if err != nil {
   318→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to generate checkpoint ID: %w", err)
   319→	}
   320→
   321→	// Determine agent type and last prompt from session
   322→	agentType := DefaultAgentType // default for backward compatibility
   323→	var lastPrompt string
   324→	if len(sessionsWithContent) > 0 {
   325→		firstSession := sessionsWithContent[0]
   326→		if firstSession.AgentType != "" {
   327→			agentType = firstSession.AgentType
   328→		}
   329→		lastPrompt = s.getLastPrompt(repo, firstSession)
   330→	}
   331→
   332→	// Prepare prompt for display: collapse newlines/whitespace, then truncate (rune-safe)
   333→	displayPrompt := stringutil.TruncateRunes(stringutil.CollapseWhitespace(lastPrompt), 80, "...")
   334→
   335→	// Add trailer differently based on commit source
   336→	switch source {
   337→	case "message":
   338→		// Using -m or -F: ask user interactively whether to add trailer
   339→		// (comments won't be stripped by git in this mode)
   340→
   341→		// Build context string for interactive prompt
   342→		var promptContext string
   343→		if displayPrompt != "" {
   344→			promptContext = "You have an active " + string(agentType) + " session.\nLast Prompt: " + displayPrompt
   345→		}
   346→
   347→		if !askConfirmTTY("Link this commit to "+string(agentType)+" session context?", promptContext, true) {
   348→			// User declined - don't add trailer
   349→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: user declined trailer",
   350→				slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   351→				slog.String("source", source),
   352→			)
   353→			return nil
   354→		}
   355→		message = addCheckpointTrailer(message, checkpointID)
   356→	default:
   357→		// Normal editor flow: add trailer with explanatory comment (will be stripped by git)
   358→		message = addCheckpointTrailerWithComment(message, checkpointID, string(agentType), displayPrompt)
   359→	}
   360→
   361→	logging.Info(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: trailer added",
   362→		slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   363→		slog.String("source", source),
   364→		slog.String("checkpoint_id", checkpointID.String()),
   365→	)
   366→
   367→	// Write updated message back
   368→	if err := os.WriteFile(commitMsgFile, []byte(message), 0o600); err != nil {
   369→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   370→	}
   371→
   372→	return nil
   373→}
   374→
   375→// handleAmendCommitMsg handles the prepare-commit-msg hook for amend operations
   376→// (source="commit"). It preserves existing trailers or restores from LastCheckpointID.
   377→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) handleAmendCommitMsg(logCtx context.Context, commitMsgFile string) error {
   378→	// Read current commit message
   379→	content, err := os.ReadFile(commitMsgFile) //nolint:gosec // commitMsgFile is provided by git hook
   380→	if err != nil {
   381→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   382→	}
   383→
   384→	message := string(content)
   385→
   386→	// If message already has a trailer, keep it unchanged
   387→	if existingCpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(message); found {
   388→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: amend preserves existing trailer",
   389→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   390→			slog.String("checkpoint_id", existingCpID.String()),
   391→		)
   392→		return nil
   393→	}
   394→
   395→	// No trailer in message — check if any session has LastCheckpointID to restore
   396→	worktreePath, err := GetWorktreePath()
   397→	if err != nil {
   398→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   399→	}
   400→
   401→	sessions, err := s.findSessionsForWorktree(worktreePath)
   402→	if err != nil || len(sessions) == 0 {
   403→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // No sessions - nothing to restore
   404→	}
   405→
   406→	// For amend, HEAD^ is the commit being amended, and HEAD is where we are now.
   407→	// We need to match sessions whose BaseCommit equals HEAD (the commit being amended
   408→	// was created from this base). This prevents stale sessions from injecting
   409→	// unrelated checkpoint IDs.
   410→	repo, repoErr := OpenRepository()
   411→	if repoErr != nil {
   412→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   413→	}
   414→	head, headErr := repo.Head()
   415→	if headErr != nil {
   416→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   417→	}
   418→	currentHead := head.Hash().String()
   419→
   420→	// Find first matching session with LastCheckpointID to restore.
   421→	// LastCheckpointID is set after condensation completes.
   422→	for _, state := range sessions {
   423→		if state.BaseCommit != currentHead {
   424→			continue
   425→		}
   426→		if state.LastCheckpointID.IsEmpty() {
   427→			continue
   428→		}
   429→		cpID := state.LastCheckpointID
   430→		source := "LastCheckpointID"
   431→
   432→		// Restore the trailer
   433→		message = addCheckpointTrailer(message, cpID)
   434→		if writeErr := os.WriteFile(commitMsgFile, []byte(message), 0o600); writeErr != nil {
   435→			return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   436→		}
   437→
   438→		logging.Info(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: restored trailer on amend",
   439→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   440→			slog.String("checkpoint_id", cpID.String()),
   441→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   442→			slog.String("source", source),
   443→		)
   444→		return nil
   445→	}
   446→
   447→	// No checkpoint ID found - leave message unchanged
   448→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: amend with no checkpoint to restore",
   449→		slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   450→	)
   451→	return nil
   452→}
   453→
   454→// PostCommit is called by the git post-commit hook after a commit is created.
   455→// Uses the session state machine to determine what action to take per session:
```
