```
    61→
    62→// InstallHooks installs Gemini CLI hooks in .gemini/settings.json.
    63→// If force is true, removes existing Entire hooks before installing.
    64→// Returns the number of hooks installed.
    65→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) InstallHooks(localDev bool, force bool) (int, error) {
    66→	// Use repo root instead of CWD to find .gemini directory
    67→	// This ensures hooks are installed correctly when run from a subdirectory
    68→	repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
    69→	if err != nil {
    70→		// Fallback to CWD if not in a git repo (e.g., during tests)
    71→		repoRoot, err = os.Getwd() //nolint:forbidigo // Intentional fallback when RepoRoot() fails (tests run outside git repos)
    72→		if err != nil {
    73→			return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to get current directory: %w", err)
    74→		}
    75→	}
    76→
    77→	settingsPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".gemini", GeminiSettingsFileName)
    78→
    79→	// Read existing settings if they exist
    80→	var rawSettings map[string]json.RawMessage
    81→
    82→	// rawHooks preserves unknown hook types
    83→	var rawHooks map[string]json.RawMessage
    84→
    85→	var hooksConfig GeminiHooksConfig
    86→
    87→	existingData, readErr := os.ReadFile(settingsPath) //nolint:gosec // path is constructed from cwd + fixed path
    88→	if readErr == nil {
    89→		if err := json.Unmarshal(existingData, &rawSettings); err != nil {
    90→			return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse existing settings.json: %w", err)
    91→		}
    92→		if hooksRaw, ok := rawSettings["hooks"]; ok {
    93→			if err := json.Unmarshal(hooksRaw, &rawHooks); err != nil {
    94→				return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse hooks in settings.json: %w", err)
    95→			}
    96→		}
    97→		if hooksConfigRaw, ok := rawSettings["hooksConfig"]; ok {
    98→			if err := json.Unmarshal(hooksConfigRaw, &hooksConfig); err != nil {
    99→				return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse hooksConfig in settings.json: %w", err)
   100→			}
   101→		}
   102→	} else {
   103→		rawSettings = make(map[string]json.RawMessage)
   104→	}
   105→
   106→	if rawHooks == nil {
   107→		rawHooks = make(map[string]json.RawMessage)
   108→	}
   109→
   110→	// Enable hooks via hooksConfig
   111→	// hooksConfig.Enabled must be true for Gemini CLI to execute hooks
   112→	hooksConfig.Enabled = true
   113→
   114→	// Define hook commands based on localDev mode
   115→	var cmdPrefix string
   116→	if localDev {
   117→		cmdPrefix = "go run ${GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR}/cmd/entire/main.go hooks gemini "
   118→	} else {
   119→		cmdPrefix = "entire hooks gemini "
   120→	}
   121→
   122→	// Parse only the hook types we need to modify
   123→	var sessionStart, sessionEnd, beforeAgent, afterAgent []GeminiHookMatcher
   124→	var beforeModel, afterModel, beforeToolSelection []GeminiHookMatcher
   125→	var beforeTool, afterTool, preCompress, notification []GeminiHookMatcher
   126→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "SessionStart", &sessionStart)
   127→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "SessionEnd", &sessionEnd)
   128→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeAgent", &beforeAgent)
   129→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "AfterAgent", &afterAgent)
   130→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeModel", &beforeModel)
   131→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "AfterModel", &afterModel)
   132→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeToolSelection", &beforeToolSelection)
   133→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeTool", &beforeTool)
   134→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "AfterTool", &afterTool)
   135→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "PreCompress", &preCompress)
   136→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "Notification", &notification)
   137→
   138→	// Check for idempotency BEFORE removing hooks
   139→	// If the exact same hook command already exists, return 0 (no changes needed)
   140→	if !force {
   141→		existingCmd := getFirstEntireHookCommand(sessionStart)
   142→		expectedCmd := cmdPrefix + "session-start"
   143→		if existingCmd == expectedCmd {
   144→			return 0, nil // Already installed with same mode
   145→		}
   146→	}
   147→
   148→	// Remove existing Entire hooks first (for clean installs and mode switching)
   149→	sessionStart = removeEntireHooks(sessionStart)
   150→	sessionEnd = removeEntireHooks(sessionEnd)
   151→	beforeAgent = removeEntireHooks(beforeAgent)
   152→	afterAgent = removeEntireHooks(afterAgent)
   153→	beforeModel = removeEntireHooks(beforeModel)
   154→	afterModel = removeEntireHooks(afterModel)
   155→	beforeToolSelection = removeEntireHooks(beforeToolSelection)
   156→	beforeTool = removeEntireHooks(beforeTool)
   157→	afterTool = removeEntireHooks(afterTool)
   158→	preCompress = removeEntireHooks(preCompress)
   159→	notification = removeEntireHooks(notification)
   160→
   161→	// Install all hooks
   162→	// Session lifecycle hooks
   163→	sessionStart = addGeminiHook(sessionStart, "", "entire-session-start", cmdPrefix+"session-start")
   164→	// SessionEnd fires on both "exit" and "logout" - install hooks for both matchers
   165→	sessionEnd = addGeminiHook(sessionEnd, "exit", "entire-session-end-exit", cmdPrefix+"session-end")
   166→	sessionEnd = addGeminiHook(sessionEnd, "logout", "entire-session-end-logout", cmdPrefix+"session-end")
   167→
   168→	// Agent hooks (user prompt and response)
   169→	beforeAgent = addGeminiHook(beforeAgent, "", "entire-before-agent", cmdPrefix+"before-agent")
   170→	afterAgent = addGeminiHook(afterAgent, "", "entire-after-agent", cmdPrefix+"after-agent")
   171→
   172→	// Model hooks (LLM request/response - fires on every LLM call)
   173→	beforeModel = addGeminiHook(beforeModel, "", "entire-before-model", cmdPrefix+"before-model")
   174→	afterModel = addGeminiHook(afterModel, "", "entire-after-model", cmdPrefix+"after-model")
   175→
   176→	// Tool selection hook (before planner selects tools)
   177→	beforeToolSelection = addGeminiHook(beforeToolSelection, "", "entire-before-tool-selection", cmdPrefix+"before-tool-selection")
   178→
   179→	// Tool hooks (before/after tool execution)
   180→	beforeTool = addGeminiHook(beforeTool, "*", "entire-before-tool", cmdPrefix+"before-tool")
   181→	afterTool = addGeminiHook(afterTool, "*", "entire-after-tool", cmdPrefix+"after-tool")
   182→
   183→	// Compression hook (before chat history compression)
   184→	preCompress = addGeminiHook(preCompress, "", "entire-pre-compress", cmdPrefix+"pre-compress")
   185→
   186→	// Notification hook (errors, warnings, info)
   187→	notification = addGeminiHook(notification, "", "entire-notification", cmdPrefix+"notification")
   188→
   189→	// 12 hooks total:
   190→	// - session-start (1)
   191→	// - session-end exit + logout (2)
   192→	// - before-agent, after-agent (2)
   193→	// - before-model, after-model (2)
   194→	// - before-tool-selection (1)
   195→	// - before-tool, after-tool (2)
   196→	// - pre-compress (1)
   197→	// - notification (1)
   198→	count := 12
   199→
   200→	// Marshal modified hook types back to rawHooks
   201→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "SessionStart", sessionStart)
   202→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "SessionEnd", sessionEnd)
   203→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeAgent", beforeAgent)
   204→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "AfterAgent", afterAgent)
   205→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeModel", beforeModel)
   206→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "AfterModel", afterModel)
   207→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeToolSelection", beforeToolSelection)
   208→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeTool", beforeTool)
   209→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "AfterTool", afterTool)
   210→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "PreCompress", preCompress)
   211→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "Notification", notification)
   212→
   213→	// Marshal hooksConfig back to raw settings
   214→	hooksConfigJSON, err := json.Marshal(hooksConfig)
   215→	if err != nil {
   216→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal hooksConfig: %w", err)
   217→	}
   218→	rawSettings["hooksConfig"] = hooksConfigJSON
   219→
   220→	// Marshal hooks back to raw settings (preserving unknown hook types)
   221→	hooksJSON, err := json.Marshal(rawHooks)
   222→	if err != nil {
   223→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal hooks: %w", err)
   224→	}
   225→	rawSettings["hooks"] = hooksJSON
   226→
   227→	// Write back to file
   228→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(settingsPath), 0o750); err != nil {
   229→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to create .gemini directory: %w", err)
   230→	}
   231→
   232→	output, err := json.MarshalIndent(rawSettings, "", "  ")
   233→	if err != nil {
   234→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal settings: %w", err)
   235→	}
   236→
   237→	if err := os.WriteFile(settingsPath, output, 0o600); err != nil {
   238→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to write settings.json: %w", err)
   239→	}
   240→
   241→	return count, nil
   242→}
   243→
   244→// parseGeminiHookType parses a specific hook type from rawHooks into the target slice.
   245→// Silently ignores parse errors (leaves target unchanged).
   246→func parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks map[string]json.RawMessage, hookType string, target *[]GeminiHookMatcher) {
   247→	if data, ok := rawHooks[hookType]; ok {
   248→		//nolint:errcheck,gosec // Intentionally ignoring parse errors - leave target as nil/empty
   249→		json.Unmarshal(data, target)
   250→	}
   251→}
   252→
   253→// marshalGeminiHookType marshals a hook type back to rawHooks.
   254→// If the slice is empty, removes the key from rawHooks.
   255→func marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks map[string]json.RawMessage, hookType string, matchers []GeminiHookMatcher) {
   256→	if len(matchers) == 0 {
   257→		delete(rawHooks, hookType)
   258→		return
   259→	}
   260→	data, err := json.Marshal(matchers)
   261→	if err != nil {
   262→		return // Silently ignore marshal errors (shouldn't happen)
   263→	}
   264→	rawHooks[hookType] = data
   265→}
   266→
   267→// UninstallHooks removes Entire hooks from Gemini CLI settings.
   268→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) UninstallHooks() error {
   269→	// Use repo root to find .gemini directory when run from a subdirectory
   270→	repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
   271→	if err != nil {
   272→		repoRoot = "." // Fallback to CWD if not in a git repo
   273→	}
   274→	settingsPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".gemini", GeminiSettingsFileName)
   275→	data, err := os.ReadFile(settingsPath) //nolint:gosec // path is constructed from repo root + fixed path
   276→	if err != nil {
   277→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // No settings file means nothing to uninstall
   278→	}
   279→
   280→	var rawSettings map[string]json.RawMessage
   281→	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &rawSettings); err != nil {
   282→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to parse settings.json: %w", err)
   283→	}
   284→
   285→	// rawHooks preserves unknown hook types
   286→	var rawHooks map[string]json.RawMessage
   287→	if hooksRaw, ok := rawSettings["hooks"]; ok {
   288→		if err := json.Unmarshal(hooksRaw, &rawHooks); err != nil {
   289→			return fmt.Errorf("failed to parse hooks: %w", err)
   290→		}
   291→	}
   292→	if rawHooks == nil {
   293→		rawHooks = make(map[string]json.RawMessage)
   294→	}
   295→
   296→	// Parse only the hook types we need to modify
   297→	var sessionStart, sessionEnd, beforeAgent, afterAgent []GeminiHookMatcher
   298→	var beforeModel, afterModel, beforeToolSelection []GeminiHookMatcher
   299→	var beforeTool, afterTool, preCompress, notification []GeminiHookMatcher
   300→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "SessionStart", &sessionStart)
   301→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "SessionEnd", &sessionEnd)
   302→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeAgent", &beforeAgent)
   303→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "AfterAgent", &afterAgent)
   304→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeModel", &beforeModel)
   305→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "AfterModel", &afterModel)
   306→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeToolSelection", &beforeToolSelection)
   307→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeTool", &beforeTool)
   308→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "AfterTool", &afterTool)
   309→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "PreCompress", &preCompress)
   310→	parseGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "Notification", &notification)
   311→
   312→	// Remove Entire hooks from all hook types
   313→	sessionStart = removeEntireHooks(sessionStart)
   314→	sessionEnd = removeEntireHooks(sessionEnd)
   315→	beforeAgent = removeEntireHooks(beforeAgent)
   316→	afterAgent = removeEntireHooks(afterAgent)
   317→	beforeModel = removeEntireHooks(beforeModel)
   318→	afterModel = removeEntireHooks(afterModel)
   319→	beforeToolSelection = removeEntireHooks(beforeToolSelection)
   320→	beforeTool = removeEntireHooks(beforeTool)
   321→	afterTool = removeEntireHooks(afterTool)
   322→	preCompress = removeEntireHooks(preCompress)
   323→	notification = removeEntireHooks(notification)
   324→
   325→	// Marshal modified hook types back to rawHooks
   326→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "SessionStart", sessionStart)
   327→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "SessionEnd", sessionEnd)
   328→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeAgent", beforeAgent)
   329→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "AfterAgent", afterAgent)
   330→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeModel", beforeModel)
   331→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "AfterModel", afterModel)
   332→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeToolSelection", beforeToolSelection)
   333→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "BeforeTool", beforeTool)
   334→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "AfterTool", afterTool)
   335→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "PreCompress", preCompress)
   336→	marshalGeminiHookType(rawHooks, "Notification", notification)
   337→
   338→	// Marshal hooks back (preserving unknown hook types)
   339→	if len(rawHooks) > 0 {
   340→		hooksJSON, err := json.Marshal(rawHooks)
   341→		if err != nil {
   342→			return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal hooks: %w", err)
   343→		}
   344→		rawSettings["hooks"] = hooksJSON
   345→	} else {
   346→		delete(rawSettings, "hooks")
   347→	}
   348→
   349→	// Write back
   350→	output, err := json.MarshalIndent(rawSettings, "", "  ")
   351→	if err != nil {
   352→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal settings: %w", err)
   353→	}
   354→
   355→	if err := os.WriteFile(settingsPath, output, 0o600); err != nil {
   356→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to write settings.json: %w", err)
   357→	}
   358→	return nil
   359→}
   360→
   361→// AreHooksInstalled checks if Entire hooks are installed.
   362→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) AreHooksInstalled() bool {
   363→	// Use repo root to find .gemini directory when run from a subdirectory
   364→	repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
   365→	if err != nil {
   366→		repoRoot = "." // Fallback to CWD if not in a git repo
   367→	}
   368→	settingsPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".gemini", GeminiSettingsFileName)
   369→	data, err := os.ReadFile(settingsPath) //nolint:gosec // path is constructed from repo root + fixed path
   370→	if err != nil {
   371→		return false
   372→	}
   373→
   374→	var settings GeminiSettings
   375→	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &settings); err != nil {
   376→		return false
   377→	}
   378→
   379→	// Check for at least one of our hooks using isEntireHook (works for both localDev and production)
   380→	return hasEntireHook(settings.Hooks.SessionStart) ||
   381→		hasEntireHook(settings.Hooks.SessionEnd) ||
   382→		hasEntireHook(settings.Hooks.BeforeAgent) ||
   383→		hasEntireHook(settings.Hooks.AfterAgent) ||
   384→		hasEntireHook(settings.Hooks.BeforeModel) ||
   385→		hasEntireHook(settings.Hooks.AfterModel) ||
   386→		hasEntireHook(settings.Hooks.BeforeToolSelection) ||
   387→		hasEntireHook(settings.Hooks.BeforeTool) ||
   388→		hasEntireHook(settings.Hooks.AfterTool) ||
   389→		hasEntireHook(settings.Hooks.PreCompress) ||
   390→		hasEntireHook(settings.Hooks.Notification)
   391→}
   392→
   393→// GetSupportedHooks returns the hook types Gemini CLI supports.
   394→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) GetSupportedHooks() []agent.HookType {
   395→	return []agent.HookType{
   396→		agent.HookSessionStart,
   397→		agent.HookSessionEnd,       // Maps to Gemini's SessionEnd (explicit exit/logout)
   398→		agent.HookStop,             // Maps to Gemini's AfterAgent (end of response)
   399→		agent.HookUserPromptSubmit, // Maps to Gemini's BeforeAgent (user prompt)
   400→		agent.HookPreToolUse,       // Maps to Gemini's BeforeTool
   401→		agent.HookPostToolUse,      // Maps to Gemini's AfterTool
   402→	}
   403→}
   404→
   405→// Helper functions for hook management
   406→
   407→// addGeminiHook adds a hook entry to matchers.
   408→// Unlike Claude Code, Gemini hooks require a "name" field.
   409→func addGeminiHook(matchers []GeminiHookMatcher, matcherName, hookName, command string) []GeminiHookMatcher {
   410→	entry := GeminiHookEntry{
   411→		Name:    hookName,
   412→		Type:    "command",
   413→		Command: command,
   414→	}
   415→
   416→	// Find or create matcher
   417→	for i, matcher := range matchers {
   418→		if matcher.Matcher == matcherName {
   419→			matchers[i].Hooks = append(matchers[i].Hooks, entry)
   420→			return matchers
   421→		}
   422→	}
   423→
   424→	// Create new matcher
   425→	newMatcher := GeminiHookMatcher{
   426→		Hooks: []GeminiHookEntry{entry},
   427→	}
   428→	if matcherName != "" {
   429→		newMatcher.Matcher = matcherName
   430→	}
   431→	return append(matchers, newMatcher)
   432→}
   433→
   434→// isEntireHook checks if a command is an Entire hook
   435→func isEntireHook(command string) bool {
   436→	for _, prefix := range entireHookPrefixes {
   437→		if strings.HasPrefix(command, prefix) {
   438→			return true
   439→		}
   440→	}
   441→	return false
   442→}
   443→
   444→// hasEntireHook checks if any hook in the matchers is an Entire hook
   445→func hasEntireHook(matchers []GeminiHookMatcher) bool {
   446→	for _, matcher := range matchers {
   447→		for _, hook := range matcher.Hooks {
   448→			if isEntireHook(hook.Command) {
   449→				return true
   450→			}
   451→		}
   452→	}
   453→	return false
   454→}
   455→
   456→// getFirstEntireHookCommand returns the command of the first Entire hook found, or empty string
   457→func getFirstEntireHookCommand(matchers []GeminiHookMatcher) string {
   458→	for _, matcher := range matchers {
   459→		for _, hook := range matcher.Hooks {
   460→			if isEntireHook(hook.Command) {
   461→				return hook.Command
   462→			}
   463→		}
   464→	}
   465→	return ""
   466→}
   467→
   468→// removeEntireHooks removes all Entire hooks from a list of matchers
   469→func removeEntireHooks(matchers []GeminiHookMatcher) []GeminiHookMatcher {
   470→	result := make([]GeminiHookMatcher, 0, len(matchers))
   471→	for _, matcher := range matchers {
   472→		filteredHooks := make([]GeminiHookEntry, 0, len(matcher.Hooks))
   473→		for _, hook := range matcher.Hooks {
   474→			if !isEntireHook(hook.Command) {
   475→				filteredHooks = append(filteredHooks, hook)
   476→			}
   477→		}
   478→		// Only keep the matcher if it has hooks remaining
   479→		if len(filteredHooks) > 0 {
   480→			matcher.Hooks = filteredHooks
   481→			result = append(result, matcher)
   482→		}
   483→	}
   484→	return result
   485→}
   486→
```

> AGENT

I see the problem. The fast path at line 282 uses `hasTTY()`:

```go
if !hasTTY() {
    for _, state := range sessions {
        if state.Phase.IsActive() {
            return s.addTrailerForAgentCommit(logCtx, commitMsgFile, state, source)
        }
    }
}
```

But `hasTTY()` can return `true` for Gemini subprocesses (they inherit the user's TTY). We only added the `GEMINI_CLI` check to `askConfirmTTY`, but not to `hasTTY()`.

When `hasTTY()` returns true, the fast path is skipped and `filterSessionsWithNewContent()` is called. For the 3rd commit (file3.go), this might fail because:
1. After file2.go's commit, the shadow branch was condensed/deleted
2. `filterSessionsWithNewContent` falls back to live transcript check
3. The transcript check might not detect file3.go yet

The fix is to add the `GEMINI_CLI` check to `hasTTY()` as well:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_015FjByYqa9hQnkGGXzU14yF
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go",
  "old_string": "func hasTTY() bool {\n\tif v := os.Getenv(\"ENTIRE_TEST_TTY\"); v != \"\" {\n\t\treturn v == \"1\"\n\t}\n\ttty, err := os.OpenFile(\"/dev/tty\", os.O_RDWR, 0)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\t_ = tty.Close()\n\treturn true\n}",
  "new_string": "func hasTTY() bool {\n\tif v := os.Getenv(\"ENTIRE_TEST_TTY\"); v != \"\" {\n\t\treturn v == \"1\"\n\t}\n\n\t// Gemini CLI sets GEMINI_CLI=1 when running shell commands.\n\t// Gemini subprocesses may have access to the user's TTY, but they can't\n\t// actually respond to interactive prompts. Treat them as non-TTY.\n\t// See: https://geminicli.com/docs/tools/shell/\n\tif os.Getenv(\"GEMINI_CLI\") != \"\" {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\n\ttty, err := os.OpenFile(\"/dev/tty\", os.O_RDWR, 0)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\t_ = tty.Close()\n\treturn true\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

Done. Now when Gemini runs `git commit`, `hasTTY()` will return `false`, triggering the fast path that always adds a trailer for ACTIVE sessions.

This fixes the issue where:
1. After committing file2.go, the shadow branch was cleaned up
2. For file3.go's commit, `filterSessionsWithNewContent()` couldn't find content (no shadow branch)
3. No trailer was added

Now the fast path will be used for all Gemini commits during an ACTIVE session, ensuring they all get checkpoint trailers.

Run the tests again:

```bash
mise run test:e2e:gemini
```

```
     1→package strategy
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"io"
     6→	"log/slog"
     7→
     8→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/logging"
     9→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5"
    10→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing"
    11→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing/object"
    12→)
    13→
    14→// Content-aware overlap detection for checkpoint management.
    15→//
    16→// These functions determine whether a commit contains session-related work by comparing
    17→// file content (not just filenames) against the shadow branch. This enables accurate
    18→// detection of the "reverted and replaced" scenario where a user:
    19→// 1. Reverts session changes (e.g., git checkout -- file.txt)
    20→// 2. Creates completely different content in the same file
    21→// 3. Commits the new content
    22→//
    23→// In this scenario, the commit should NOT get a checkpoint trailer because the
    24→// session's work was discarded, not incorporated.
    25→//
    26→// The key distinction:
    27→// - Modified files (exist in parent commit): Always count as overlap, regardless of
    28→//   content changes. The user is editing session's work.
    29→// - New files (don't exist in parent): Require content match against shadow branch.
    30→//   If content differs completely, the session's work was likely reverted & replaced.
    31→
    32→// filesOverlapWithContent checks if any file in filesTouched overlaps with the committed
    33→// content, using content-aware comparison to detect the "reverted and replaced" scenario.
    34→//
    35→// This is used in PostCommit to determine if a session has work in the commit.
    36→func filesOverlapWithContent(repo *git.Repository, shadowBranchName string, headCommit *object.Commit, filesTouched []string) bool {
    37→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
    38→
    39→	// Build set of filesTouched for quick lookup
    40→	touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
    41→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
    42→		touchedSet[f] = true
    43→	}
    44→
    45→	// Get HEAD commit tree (the committed content)
    46→	headTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
    47→	if err != nil {
    48→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD tree, falling back to filename check",
    49→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    50→		)
    51→		return len(filesTouched) > 0 // Fall back: assume overlap if any files touched
    52→	}
    53→
    54→	// Get shadow branch tree (the session's content)
    55→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
    56→	shadowRef, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
    57→	if err != nil {
    58→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: shadow branch not found, falling back to filename check",
    59→			slog.String("branch", shadowBranchName),
    60→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    61→		)
    62→		return len(filesTouched) > 0 // Fall back: assume overlap if any files touched
    63→	}
    64→
    65→	shadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(shadowRef.Hash())
    66→	if err != nil {
    67→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get shadow commit, falling back to filename check",
    68→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    69→		)
    70→		return len(filesTouched) > 0
    71→	}
    72→
    73→	shadowTree, err := shadowCommit.Tree()
    74→	if err != nil {
    75→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get shadow tree, falling back to filename check",
    76→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    77→		)
    78→		return len(filesTouched) > 0
    79→	}
    80→
    81→	// Get the parent commit tree to determine if files are modified vs newly created.
    82→	// For modified files (exist in parent), we count as overlap regardless of content
    83→	// because the user is editing the session's work.
    84→	// For newly created files (don't exist in parent), we check content to detect
    85→	// the "reverted and replaced" scenario where user deleted session's work and
    86→	// created something completely different.
    87→	var parentTree *object.Tree
    88→	if headCommit.NumParents() > 0 {
    89→		if parent, err := headCommit.Parent(0); err == nil {
    90→			if pTree, err := parent.Tree(); err == nil {
    91→				parentTree = pTree
    92→			}
    93→		}
    94→	}
    95→
    96→	// Check each file in filesTouched
    97→	for _, filePath := range filesTouched {
    98→		// Get file from HEAD tree (the committed content)
    99→		headFile, err := headTree.File(filePath)
   100→		if err != nil {
   101→			// File not in HEAD commit. This happens when:
   102→			// - The session created/modified the file but user deleted it before committing
   103→			// - The file was staged as a deletion (git rm)
   104→			// In both cases, the session's work on this file is not in the commit,
   105→			// so it doesn't contribute to overlap. Continue checking other files.
   106→			continue
   107→		}
   108→
   109→		// Check if this is a modified file (exists in parent) or new file
   110→		isModified := false
   111→		if parentTree != nil {
   112→			if _, err := parentTree.File(filePath); err == nil {
   113→				isModified = true
   114→			}
   115→		}
   116→
   117→		// Modified files always count as overlap (user edited session's work)
   118→		if isModified {
   119→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: modified file counts as overlap",
   120→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   121→			)
   122→			return true
   123→		}
   124→
   125→		// For new files, check content against shadow branch
   126→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(filePath)
   127→		if err != nil {
   128→			// File not in shadow branch - this shouldn't happen but skip it
   129→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: file in filesTouched but not in shadow branch",
   130→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   131→			)
   132→			continue
   133→		}
   134→
   135→		// Compare by hash (blob hash) - exact content match required for new files
   136→		if headFile.Hash == shadowFile.Hash {
   137→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: new file content match found",
   138→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   139→				slog.String("hash", headFile.Hash.String()),
   140→			)
   141→			return true
   142→		}
   143→
   144→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: new file content mismatch (may be reverted & replaced)",
   145→			slog.String("file", filePath),
   146→			slog.String("head_hash", headFile.Hash.String()),
   147→			slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()),
   148→		)
   149→	}
   150→
   151→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: no overlapping files found",
   152→		slog.Int("files_checked", len(filesTouched)),
   153→	)
   154→	return false
   155→}
   156→
   157→// stagedFilesOverlapWithContent checks if any staged file overlaps with filesTouched,
   158→// distinguishing between modified files (always overlap) and new files (check content).
   159→//
   160→// For modified files (already exist in HEAD), we count as overlap because the user
   161→// is editing the session's work. For new files (don't exist in HEAD), we require
   162→// content match to detect the "reverted and replaced" scenario.
   163→//
   164→// This is used in PrepareCommitMsg for carry-forward scenarios.
   165→func stagedFilesOverlapWithContent(repo *git.Repository, shadowTree *object.Tree, stagedFiles, filesTouched []string) bool {
   166→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   167→
   168→	// Build set of filesTouched for quick lookup
   169→	touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
   170→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
   171→		touchedSet[f] = true
   172→	}
   173→
   174→	// Get HEAD tree to determine if files are being modified or newly created
   175→	head, err := repo.Head()
   176→	if err != nil {
   177→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD, falling back to filename check",
   178→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   179→		)
   180→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   181→	}
   182→	headCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(head.Hash())
   183→	if err != nil {
   184→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD commit, falling back to filename check",
   185→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   186→		)
   187→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   188→	}
   189→	headTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
   190→	if err != nil {
   191→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD tree, falling back to filename check",
   192→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   193→		)
   194→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   195→	}
   196→
   197→	// Get the git index to access staged file hashes
   198→	idx, err := repo.Storer.Index()
   199→	if err != nil {
   200→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get index, falling back to filename check",
   201→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   202→		)
   203→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   204→	}
   205→
   206→	// Build a map of index entries for O(1) lookup (avoid O(n*m) nested loop)
   207→	indexEntries := make(map[string]plumbing.Hash, len(idx.Entries))
   208→	for _, entry := range idx.Entries {
   209→		indexEntries[entry.Name] = entry.Hash
   210→	}
   211→
   212→	// Check each staged file
   213→	for _, stagedPath := range stagedFiles {
   214→		if !touchedSet[stagedPath] {
   215→			continue // Not in filesTouched, skip
   216→		}
   217→
   218→		// Check if this is a modified file (exists in HEAD) or new file
   219→		_, headErr := headTree.File(stagedPath)
   220→		isModified := headErr == nil
   221→
   222→		// Modified files always count as overlap (user edited session's work)
   223→		if isModified {
   224→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: modified file counts as overlap",
   225→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   226→			)
   227→			return true
   228→		}
   229→
   230→		// For new files, check content against shadow branch
   231→		stagedHash, found := indexEntries[stagedPath]
   232→		if !found {
   233→			continue // Not in index (shouldn't happen but be safe)
   234→		}
   235→
   236→		// Get file from shadow branch tree
   237→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(stagedPath)
   238→		if err != nil {
   239→			// File not in shadow branch - can't verify content overlap.
   240→			// Don't assume overlap just because it's in filesTouched.
   241→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: file not in shadow tree, skipping",
   242→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   243→			)
   244→			continue
   245→		}
   246→
   247→		// Compare hashes - exact match means file is unchanged
   248→		if stagedHash == shadowFile.Hash {
   249→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: new file content match found",
   250→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   251→				slog.String("hash", stagedHash.String()),
   252→			)
   253→			return true
   254→		}
   255→
   256→		// Hashes differ - check if there's significant content overlap.
   257→		// This distinguishes partial staging (user kept some agent content) from
   258→		// "reverted and replaced" (user wrote completely different content).
   259→		shadowContent, shadowErr := shadowFile.Contents()
   260→		if shadowErr != nil {
   261→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to read shadow content",
   262→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   263→				slog.String("error", shadowErr.Error()),
   264→			)
   265→			continue
   266→		}
   267→
   268→		// Read staged content from object store
   269→		stagedBlob, blobErr := repo.BlobObject(stagedHash)
   270→		if blobErr != nil {
   271→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to read staged blob",
   272→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   273→				slog.String("error", blobErr.Error()),
   274→			)
   275→			continue
   276→		}
   277→		stagedReader, readerErr := stagedBlob.Reader()
   278→		if readerErr != nil {
   279→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get staged reader",
   280→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   281→				slog.String("error", readerErr.Error()),
   282→			)
   283→			continue
   284→		}
   285→		stagedBytes, readErr := io.ReadAll(stagedReader)
   286→		_ = stagedReader.Close() // Best effort close
   287→		if readErr != nil {
   288→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to read staged content",
   289→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   290→				slog.String("error", readErr.Error()),
   291→			)
   292→			continue
   293→		}
   294→		stagedContent := string(stagedBytes)
   295→
   296→		// Check for significant content overlap
   297→		if hasSignificantContentOverlap(stagedContent, shadowContent) {
   298→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: new file has partial overlap (partial staging)",
   299→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   300→			)
   301→			return true
   302→		}
   303→
   304→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: new file has no significant overlap (reverted & replaced)",
   305→			slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   306→			slog.String("staged_hash", stagedHash.String()),
   307→			slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()),
   308→		)
   309→	}
   310→
   311→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: no overlapping files found",
   312→		slog.Int("staged_files", len(stagedFiles)),
   313→		slog.Int("files_touched", len(filesTouched)),
   314→	)
   315→	return false
   316→}
   317→
   318→// hasOverlappingFiles checks if any file in stagedFiles appears in filesTouched.
   319→// This is a fallback when content-aware comparison isn't possible.
   320→func hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched []string) bool {
   321→	touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
   322→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
   323→		touchedSet[f] = true
   324→	}
   325→
   326→	for _, staged := range stagedFiles {
   327→		if touchedSet[staged] {
   328→			return true
   329→		}
   330→	}
   331→	return false
   332→}
   333→
   334→// filesWithRemainingAgentChanges returns files from filesTouched that still have
   335→// uncommitted agent changes. This is used for carry-forward after partial commits.
   336→//
   337→// A file has remaining agent changes if:
   338→//   - It wasn't committed at all (not in committedFiles), OR
   339→//   - It was committed but the committed content doesn't match the shadow branch
   340→//     (user committed partial changes, e.g., via git add -p)
   341→//
   342→// Falls back to file-level subtraction if shadow branch is unavailable.
   343→func filesWithRemainingAgentChanges(
   344→	repo *git.Repository,
   345→	shadowBranchName string,
   346→	headCommit *object.Commit,
   347→	filesTouched []string,
   348→	committedFiles map[string]struct{},
   349→) []string {
   350→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   351→
   352→	// Get HEAD commit tree (the committed content)
   353→	commitTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
   354→	if err != nil {
   355→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: failed to get commit tree, falling back to file subtraction",
   356→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   357→		)
   358→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   359→	}
   360→
   361→	// Get shadow branch tree (the session's full content)
   362→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   363→	shadowRef, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
   364→	if err != nil {
   365→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: shadow branch not found, falling back to file subtraction",
   366→			slog.String("branch", shadowBranchName),
   367→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   368→		)
   369→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   370→	}
   371→
   372→	shadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(shadowRef.Hash())
   373→	if err != nil {
   374→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: failed to get shadow commit, falling back to file subtraction",
   375→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   376→		)
   377→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   378→	}
   379→
   380→	shadowTree, err := shadowCommit.Tree()
   381→	if err != nil {
   382→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: failed to get shadow tree, falling back to file subtraction",
   383→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   384→		)
   385→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   386→	}
   387→
   388→	var remaining []string
   389→
   390→	for _, filePath := range filesTouched {
   391→		// If file wasn't committed at all, it definitely has remaining changes
   392→		if _, wasCommitted := committedFiles[filePath]; !wasCommitted {
   393→			remaining = append(remaining, filePath)
   394→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file not committed, keeping",
   395→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   396→			)
   397→			continue
   398→		}
   399→
   400→		// File was committed - check if committed content matches shadow branch
   401→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(filePath)
   402→		if err != nil {
   403→			// File not in shadow branch - nothing to carry forward for this file
   404→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file not in shadow branch, skipping",
   405→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   406→			)
   407→			continue
   408→		}
   409→
   410→		commitFile, err := commitTree.File(filePath)
   411→		if err != nil {
   412→			// File not in commit tree (deleted?) - keep it if it's in shadow
   413→			remaining = append(remaining, filePath)
   414→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file not in commit tree but in shadow, keeping",
   415→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   416→			)
   417→			continue
   418→		}
   419→
   420→		// Compare hashes - if different, there are still uncommitted agent changes
   421→		if commitFile.Hash != shadowFile.Hash {
   422→			remaining = append(remaining, filePath)
   423→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: content mismatch, keeping for carry-forward",
   424→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   425→				slog.String("commit_hash", commitFile.Hash.String()[:7]),
   426→				slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()[:7]),
   427→			)
   428→		} else {
   429→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: content fully committed",
   430→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   431→			)
   432→		}
   433→	}
   434→
   435→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: result",
   436→		slog.Int("files_touched", len(filesTouched)),
   437→		slog.Int("committed_files", len(committedFiles)),
   438→		slog.Int("remaining_files", len(remaining)),
   439→	)
   440→
   441→	return remaining
   442→}
   443→
   444→// subtractFilesByName returns files from filesTouched that are NOT in committedFiles.
   445→// This is a fallback when content-aware comparison isn't possible.
   446→func subtractFilesByName(filesTouched []string, committedFiles map[string]struct{}) []string {
   447→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   448→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "subtractFilesByName: ",
   449→		slog.Any("filesTouched", filesTouched),
   450→		slog.Any("committedFiles", committedFiles),
   451→	)
   452→	var remaining []string
   453→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
   454→		if _, committed := committedFiles[f]; !committed {
   455→			remaining = append(remaining, f)
   456→		}
   457→	}
   458→	return remaining
   459→}
   460→
   461→// hasSignificantContentOverlap checks if two file contents share significant lines.
   462→// This distinguishes partial staging (user kept some agent content) from
   463→// "reverted and replaced" (user wrote completely different content).
   464→//
   465→// For larger files, we require at least 2 matching lines because a single match
   466→// (like "package main") is likely common boilerplate. For small files (≤ 2
   467→// significant lines in either file), any single match counts as overlap since
   468→// there aren't many lines to match anyway.
   469→//
   470→// The function filters out trivial lines (short lines < 10 chars like braces,
   471→// empty lines, etc.) because these commonly appear in many files and don't
   472→// indicate meaningful overlap.
   473→func hasSignificantContentOverlap(stagedContent, shadowContent string) bool {
   474→	// Build set of significant lines from shadow (agent) content
   475→	shadowLines := extractSignificantLines(shadowContent)
   476→
   477→	// Build set of significant lines from staged (user) content
   478→	stagedLines := extractSignificantLines(stagedContent)
   479→
   480→	// If either has no significant lines, no meaningful overlap possible
   481→	if len(shadowLines) == 0 || len(stagedLines) == 0 {
   482→		return false
   483→	}
   484→
   485→	// For very small files (0-1 significant lines), any single match is meaningful
   486→	// since requiring 2 matches would be impossible. The 2-line requirement is
   487→	// mainly to filter out coincidental boilerplate (like "package main") in
   488→	// larger files where we CAN require multiple matches.
   489→	isVerySmallFile := len(shadowLines) < 2 || len(stagedLines) < 2
   490→	requiredMatches := 2
   491→	if isVerySmallFile {
   492→		requiredMatches = 1
   493→	}
   494→
   495→	// Count matching lines
   496→	matchCount := 0
   497→	for line := range stagedLines {
   498→		if shadowLines[line] {
   499→			matchCount++
   500→			if matchCount >= requiredMatches {
   501→				return true
   502→			}
   503→		}
   504→	}
   505→
   506→	return false
   507→}
   508→
   509→// extractSignificantLines returns a set of significant lines from content.
   510→// Lines are trimmed and must be at least 10 characters.
   511→//
   512→// This simple length-based filter works well because:
   513→// - Most structural boilerplate is short: `{`, `}`, `});`, `} else {` (all < 10 chars)
   514→// - Long lines (>= 10 chars) are usually meaningful regardless of language
   515→// - No language-specific pattern matching needed
   516→func extractSignificantLines(content string) map[string]bool {
   517→	lines := make(map[string]bool)
   518→	for _, line := range splitLines([]byte(content)) {
   519→		trimmed := trimLine(line)
   520→		if len(trimmed) >= 10 {
   521→			lines[trimmed] = true
   522→		}
   523→	}
   524→	return lines
   525→}
   526→
   527→// trimLine removes leading and trailing whitespace from a line.
   528→func trimLine(line string) string {
   529→	start := 0
   530→	end := len(line)
   531→	for start < end && (line[start] == ' ' || line[start] == '\t') {
   532→		start++
   533→	}
   534→	for end > start && (line[end-1] == ' ' || line[end-1] == '\t') {
   535→		end--
   536→	}
   537→	return line[start:end]
   538→}
   539→
```

> AGENT

I see the issue now. Looking at `filesWithRemainingAgentChanges`:

```go
for _, filePath := range filesTouched {
    // If file wasn't committed at all, it definitely has remaining changes
    if _, wasCommitted := committedFiles[filePath]; !wasCommitted {
        remaining = append(remaining, filePath)  // ← BUG: doesn't check if already in HEAD
        continue
    }
    // ...
}
```

When a file is not in **this** commit's `committedFiles`, it's automatically added to `remaining` without checking if it was already committed in a **previous** commit. This causes files from earlier commits to be incorrectly carried forward.

The fix is to check if the file already exists in HEAD with matching content before adding it to remaining:

```json
  "new_string": "\tfor _, filePath := range filesTouched {\n\t\t// If file wasn't committed in THIS commit, check if it's already in HEAD\n\t\t// (committed in a previous commit). If content matches shadow branch,\n\t\t// it's fully committed and doesn't need carry-forward.\n\t\tif _, wasCommitted := committedFiles[filePath]; !wasCommitted {\n\t\t\t// Check if file exists in HEAD (commit tree)\n\t\t\theadFile, headErr := commitTree.File(filePath)\n\t\t\tif headErr == nil {\n\t\t\t\t// File exists in HEAD - check if it matches shadow branch\n\t\t\t\tshadowFile, shadowErr := shadowTree.File(filePath)\n\t\t\t\tif shadowErr == nil && headFile.Hash == shadowFile.Hash {\n\t\t\t\t\t// Content matches - file was fully committed in a previous commit\n\t\t\t\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file already in HEAD with matching content, skipping\",\n\t\t\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"file\", filePath),\n\t\t\t\t\t)\n\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t// File not in HEAD or content doesn't match shadow - needs carry-forward\n\t\t\tremaining = append(remaining, filePath)\n\t\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file not committed, keeping\",\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"file\", filePath),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

question: this test works for claude code, but fails for gemini, does this make sense with your analysis?

> AGENT

Good question. Let me think about this...

The key difference is `hasTTY()`. For Claude Code:
- Claude subprocesses don't have access to `/dev/tty` (truly non-interactive)
- `hasTTY()` returns `false`
- The **fast path** in `PrepareCommitMsg` is always taken for ACTIVE sessions
- `addTrailerForAgentCommit` is called directly

For Gemini (before my fix):
- Gemini subprocesses inherit the user's TTY (even though they can't respond)
- `hasTTY()` returned `true`
- The fast path was skipped
- `filterSessionsWithNewContent()` was called, which uses different logic

My `hasTTY()` fix makes Gemini behave like Claude for **PrepareCommitMsg** (checkpoint trailer creation), which is why we now get 3 checkpoints.

But the shadow branch cleanup issue is in **PostCommit**, which runs after the commit. Let me check if the carry-forward logic is also affected by something agent-specific.

```
   469→
   470→// PostCommit is called by the git post-commit hook after a commit is created.
   471→// Uses the session state machine to determine what action to take per session:
   472→//   - ACTIVE → condense immediately (each commit gets its own checkpoint)
   473→//   - IDLE → condense immediately
   474→//   - ENDED → condense if files touched, discard if empty
   475→//
   476→// After condensation for ACTIVE sessions, remaining uncommitted files are
   477→// carried forward to a new shadow branch so the next commit gets its own checkpoint.
   478→//
   479→// Shadow branches are only deleted when ALL sessions sharing the branch are non-active
   480→// and were condensed during this PostCommit.
   481→
   482→// postCommitActionHandler implements session.ActionHandler for PostCommit.
   483→// Each session in the loop gets its own handler with per-session context.
   484→// Handler methods use the *State parameter from ApplyTransition (same pointer
   485→// as the state being transitioned) rather than capturing state separately.
   486→type postCommitActionHandler struct {
   487→	s                      *ManualCommitStrategy
   488→	logCtx                 context.Context
   489→	repo                   *git.Repository
   490→	checkpointID           id.CheckpointID
   491→	head                   *plumbing.Reference
   492→	commit                 *object.Commit
   493→	newHead                string
   494→	shadowBranchName       string
   495→	shadowBranchesToDelete map[string]struct{}
   496→	committedFileSet       map[string]struct{}
   497→	hasNew                 bool
   498→
   499→	// Output: set by handler methods, read by caller after TransitionAndLog.
   500→	condensed bool
   501→}
   502→
   503→func (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleCondense(state *session.State) error {
   504→	// For ACTIVE sessions, any commit during the turn is session-related.
   505→	// For IDLE/ENDED sessions (e.g., carry-forward), also require that the
   506→	// committed files overlap with the session's remaining files AND have
   507→	// matching content — otherwise an unrelated commit (or a commit with
   508→	// completely replaced content) would incorrectly get this session's checkpoint.
   509→	shouldCondense := h.hasNew
   510→	if shouldCondense && !state.Phase.IsActive() {
   511→		shouldCondense = filesOverlapWithContent(h.repo, h.shadowBranchName, h.commit, state.FilesTouched)
   512→	}
   513→
   514→	logging.Debug(h.logCtx, "post-commit: HandleCondense decision",
   515→		slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   516→		slog.String("phase", string(state.Phase)),
   517→		slog.Bool("has_new", h.hasNew),
   518→		slog.Bool("should_condense", shouldCondense),
   519→		slog.String("shadow_branch", h.shadowBranchName),
   520→	)
   521→
   522→	if shouldCondense {
   523→		h.condensed = h.s.condenseAndUpdateState(h.logCtx, h.repo, h.checkpointID, state, h.head, h.shadowBranchName, h.shadowBranchesToDelete, h.committedFileSet)
   524→	} else {
   525→		h.s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(h.logCtx, state, h.newHead)
   526→	}
   527→	return nil
   528→}
   529→
   530→func (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleCondenseIfFilesTouched(state *session.State) error {
   531→	shouldCondense := len(state.FilesTouched) > 0 && h.hasNew
   532→
   533→	logging.Debug(h.logCtx, "post-commit: HandleCondenseIfFilesTouched decision",
   534→		slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   535→		slog.String("phase", string(state.Phase)),
   536→		slog.Bool("has_new", h.hasNew),
   537→		slog.Int("files_touched", len(state.FilesTouched)),
   538→		slog.Bool("should_condense", shouldCondense),
   539→		slog.String("shadow_branch", h.shadowBranchName),
   540→	)
   541→
   542→	if shouldCondense {
   543→		h.condensed = h.s.condenseAndUpdateState(h.logCtx, h.repo, h.checkpointID, state, h.head, h.shadowBranchName, h.shadowBranchesToDelete, h.committedFileSet)
   544→	} else {
   545→		h.s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(h.logCtx, state, h.newHead)
   546→	}
   547→	return nil
   548→}
   549→
   550→func (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleDiscardIfNoFiles(state *session.State) error {
   551→	if len(state.FilesTouched) == 0 {
   552→		logging.Debug(h.logCtx, "post-commit: skipping empty ended session (no files to condense)",
   553→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   554→		)
   555→	}
   556→	h.s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(h.logCtx, state, h.newHead)
   557→	return nil
   558→}
   559→
   560→func (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleWarnStaleSession(_ *session.State) error {
   561→	// Not produced by EventGitCommit; no-op for exhaustiveness.
   562→	return nil
   563→}
   564→
   565→// During rebase/cherry-pick/revert operations, phase transitions are skipped entirely.
   566→//
   567→//nolint:unparam // error return required by interface but hooks must return nil
   568→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) PostCommit() error {
   569→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   570→
   571→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
   572→	if err != nil {
   573→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   574→	}
   575→
   576→	// Get HEAD commit to check for trailer
   577→	head, err := repo.Head()
   578→	if err != nil {
   579→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   580→	}
   581→
   582→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(head.Hash())
   583→	if err != nil {
   584→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   585→	}
   586→
   587→	// Check if commit has checkpoint trailer (ParseCheckpoint validates format)
   588→	checkpointID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(commit.Message)
   589→	if !found {
   590→		// No trailer — user removed it or it was never added (mid-turn commit).
   591→		// Still update BaseCommit for active sessions so future commits can match.
   592→		s.postCommitUpdateBaseCommitOnly(logCtx, head)
   593→		return nil
   594→	}
   595→
   596→	worktreePath, err := GetWorktreePath()
   597→	if err != nil {
   598→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   599→	}
```

> AGENT

Looking at the code, I see the issue more clearly now. The key is `extractFilesFromLiveTranscript` at line 668-670:

```go
if len(filesTouchedBefore) == 0 && state.Phase.IsActive() && state.TranscriptPath != "" {
    filesTouchedBefore = s.extractFilesFromLiveTranscript(state)
}
```

This is called only when `state.FilesTouched` is empty. For subsequent commits, `state.FilesTouched` should contain only the carry-forward files from the previous commit.

But wait - are the Claude Code E2E tests even running this same multi-commit scenario? Let me check:

Found it! Line 1864:
```go
state.CheckpointTranscriptStart = 0
```

In `carryForwardToNewShadowBranch`, `CheckpointTranscriptStart` is reset to 0. But this only matters when `extractFilesFromLiveTranscript` is called next time.

> AGENT

Gemini's `ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset` correctly respects the offset (line 336: `for i := startOffset`). So that's not the issue.

Let me think about what's different between Claude and Gemini...

Looking at the flow, after file2 commit:
1. Carry-forward creates shadow branch at file2's hash with file3 content
2. `state.FilesTouched = [file3]`
3. State is saved

For file3 commit:
1. `state.FilesTouched = [file3]` → NOT empty, don't extract from transcript
2. `remainingFiles = []` (file3 was committed)
3. Shadow branch at file2's hash should be deleted

This flow seems correct for both agents. But the test shows the branch still exists for Gemini...

One possibility: **Gemini's transcript might not be fully flushed to disk** when PostCommit runs. The transcript is written by Gemini CLI, and if it's buffered, `ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset` might not see all files, leading to different behavior.

Could you add some debug logging to see what `state.FilesTouched` contains at each commit? Or run the test with `-v` to see more details?

Alternatively, there might be a timing issue where the Gemini hooks fire in a different order than Claude's. The test output shows:
```
Hook execution for SessionEnd: 1 hooks executed successfully
Hook execution for SessionEnd: 1 hooks executed successfully
```

Two SessionEnd hooks (for "exit" and "logout" matchers) - this is expected but could interact unexpectedly with the state.
